\|\theta-\theta_{0}\|_{\infty}\leq\eta^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\hat{\Psi}_{n}(\theta)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varphi(X_{i})\rho\bigl(\varepsilon_{i}+\bigl\{e_{0}(X_{i})+Q_{h}(X_{i}-x_{0})^{\top}(\theta_{0}-\theta)\bigr\}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{x_{0}}(h)}(X_{i})\,\big|\,X_{i}\bigr)+o_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{X},\mathcal{H},\Lambda}(1)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varphi(X_{i})\rho\bigl(\varepsilon_{i}+Q_{h}(X_{i}-x_{0})^{\top}(\theta_{0}-\theta)\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{x_{0}}(h)}(X_{i})\,\big|\,X_{i}\bigr)+o_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{X},\mathcal{H},\Lambda}(1)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl\{K_{h}(X-x_{0})\rho\bigl(\varepsilon+Q_{h}(X-x_{0})^{\top}(\theta_{0}-\theta)\,\big|\,X\bigr)\,\big|\,X=x_{0}\bigr\}+o_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{X},\mathcal{H},\Lambda}(1)

\displaystyle=\Psi(\theta)+o_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{X},\mathcal{H},\Lambda}(1).

Hence the unique root
\hat{\theta}^{*}
of
\hat{\Psi}_{n}
in
\{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{\bar{p}}:\|\theta-\theta_{0}\|_{\infty}\leq\eta_{2}\}
satisfies
\hat{\theta}^{*}=\theta_{0}+o_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{X},\mathcal{H},\Lambda}(1)
.
It remains to show that
\hat{\theta}=\hat{\theta}^{*}
with probability at least
1-o_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{X},\mathcal{H},\Lambda}(1)
.
Recall from Algorithm
1
that
\hat{\theta}^{\mathrm{LP}}
is the unique vector of coefficients that solves the local polynomial estimating equations, and let
\hat{\theta}^{c}
be a root on the outer region
\{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{\bar{p}}:\|\theta-\theta_{0}\|_{\infty}>\eta_{2}\}
if at least one exists. Then

\|\hat{\theta}^{c}-\hat{\theta}^{\mathrm{LP}}\|_{\infty}\geq\|\hat{\theta}^{c}-\theta_{0}\|_{\infty}-\|\hat{\theta}^{\mathrm{LP}}-\theta_{0}\|_{\infty}>\eta_{2}-o_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{X},\mathcal{H},\Lambda}(1).

On the other hand, by assumption

\|\hat{\theta}-\hat{\theta}^{\mathrm{LP}}\|_{\infty}\leq\eta_{1},

so on a sequence of events of probability at least
1-o_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{X},\mathcal{H},\Lambda}(1)
, we have
\hat{\theta}^{*}=\hat{\theta}
, as required.
∎

Lemma 21
.

Under Assumptions
A1
and
A2
, define a standard local polynomial estimator
\hat{f}^{\mathrm{LP}}
by

\hat{f}^{\mathrm{LP}}(x):=\biggl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}K_{h}(X_{i}-x)Q_{h}(X_{i}-x)Q_{h}(X_{i}-x)^{\top}\biggr)^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}K_{h}(X_{i}-x)Q_{h}(X_{i}-x)Y_{i}.

Then

\mathbb{E}_{P}\Bigl\{\bigl(\hat{f}^{\mathrm{LP}}(x_{0})-f(x_{0})\bigr)^{2}\Bigr\}=O_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{X},\mathcal{H},\Lambda}\biggl(h^{2\beta^{*}}+\frac{1}{nh^{d}}\biggr).

Proof.

Algorithm
1
can trivially be adapted to construct the simpler standard local polynomial estimator
\hat{f}^{\mathrm{LP}}
by removing the for loop over
k\in[\mathcal{K}]
in entirety, and instead taking
\hat{f}^{\mathrm{LP}}_{k}=\hat{\mu}_{k}=\hat{c}_{k}\equiv 0
for all
k\in[\mathcal{K}]
and also taking
\kappa_{\lambda}\equiv 0
and
\hat{\rho}_{k}=\rho=\mathrm{id}
for all
k\in[\mathcal{K}]
. In the decomposition (
38
),
\mathrm{e}_{1}^{\top}\Omega^{-1}\hat{\phi}_{\textup{III}}(\boldsymbol{\tau})={e}_{1}^{\top}\Omega^{-1}\hat{\phi}_{\textup{IV}}(\boldsymbol{\tau})=0
. Further, in the proof of Lemma
8
,
\hat{\phi}_{\textup{I}.\mathrm{i}.k}=\hat{\phi}_{\textup{I}.\mathrm{ii}.k}=\hat{\phi}_{\textup{I}.\mathrm{iii}.k}=0
, and in the proof of Lemma
11
,
\hat{\phi}_{\textup{II}.\mathrm{iii}.k}=0
for all
k\in[\mathcal{K}]
. The remaining three terms
\phi_{\textup{I}}
,
\phi_{\textup{II}.\mathrm{i}}^{*}
, and
\hat{\phi}_{\textup{II}.\mathrm{ii}.k}
are dealt with as in Lemmas
8
and
11
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
7
.

Theorem
7
follows by identical arguments to the proof of Theorem
1
, with the exception of two small amendments that we now describe. In (
45
), by similar arguments we have

\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigg\{\bigg(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\kappa_{h,\lambda}(X_{i}-x_{0})\varrho(\varepsilon_{i}\,|\,X_{i})\bigg)^{2}\bigg\}=o_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{X},\mathcal{H},\Lambda}\biggl(\frac{1}{nh^{d}}+h^{2\beta^{*}}\biggr),

and so writing
\hat{\phi}_{\textup{I}.\mathrm{i}.k}[\varrho]
for the quantity
\hat{\phi}_{\textup{I}.\mathrm{i}.k}
in (
44
) with
\rho
replaced with
\varrho
, we find that
\eta^{\top}\hat{\phi}_{\textup{I}.\mathrm{i}.k}[\varrho]=o_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{X},\mathcal{H},\Lambda}\bigl(\frac{1}{\sqrt{nh^{d}}}+h^{\beta^{*}}\bigr)
. Second, writing
Z_{n,1}[\varrho]
for the quantity
Z_{n,1}
in (
67
) with
\rho
replaced with
\varrho
, by (
71
) and (
23
),

\frac{\mathbb{E}_{P}\big(|Z_{n,1}[\varrho]|^{2+\delta}\big)}{n^{\delta/2}\sigma_{P}^{2+\delta}}=O_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{X},\mathcal{H},\Lambda}\biggl(\frac{1}{n^{\delta/2}}\biggl\{1+h^{(2\beta+d)(1+\delta/2)}\biggr\}\biggr)=o_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{X},\mathcal{H},\Lambda}(1).

The result follows.
∎

Appendix B
Existence of higher order kernels

From a methodological standpoint, it would be common to consider local polynomial estimators (e.g. locally linear polynomials) with a second order kernel
K
. However, from a theoretical perspective, our results allow for estimation of functions of any Hölder smoothness
\beta\in(0,\infty)
. Assumption
(A2.1)
, which is used in Theorem
1
, asks for a
d
-dimensional kernel
K
of order
p+1
for arbitrary
d\in\mathbb{N},p\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
with
s_{1}(K)
invertible. We establish the existence of such a kernel below. It is convenient to let
\bar{p}^{*}:=\binom{d+2p}{2p}
and define
Q^{\mathrm{ext}}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{\bar{p}^{*}}
by
Q^{\mathrm{ext}}(u):=\bigl(\frac{1}{\alpha!}\prod_{r=1}^{d}u_{r}^{\alpha_{r}}\bigr)_{\|\alpha\|_{1}\leq 2p}
, with components ordered in increasing graded lexicographic order in
\alpha
i.e. for
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
we say
u^{\alpha_{1}}<_{\mathrm{gr.lex}}u^{\alpha_{2}}
if
\|\alpha_{1}\|_{1}<\|\alpha_{2}\|_{1}
or
\|\alpha_{1}\|_{1}=\|\alpha_{2}\|_{1}
and
\alpha_{1}<_{\mathrm{lex}}\alpha_{2}
, where
<_{\mathrm{lex}}
denotes the lexicographic order. It will also help to define the shorthand
\mathrm{C}^{n}_{r}:={\binom{n}{r}}=\frac{n!}{r!(n-r)!}
. Our proof will also rest on a construction involving a set of algebraically independent numbers
(
37
, e.g.)
. The real numbers
\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{n}\in\mathbb{R}
are
algebraically independent over
\mathbb{Q}
if no non-trivial polynomial in
\mathbb{Q}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}]
vanishes on
(\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{n})
.
For a block matrix
M\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
of the form

M:=\begin{pmatrix}A&B^{\top}\\
B&C\end{pmatrix},

where
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}\times n_{1}},B\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{2}\times n_{1}}
and
C\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{2}\times n_{2}}
with
n_{1}+n_{2}=n
with
C
invertible, define the Schur complement of the block
C
with respect to
M
is defined as
A-B^{\top}C^{-1}B
. We will use the result that
M
is invertible if the Schur complement of the block
C
with respect to
M
is invertible
(see e.g.
47
, Proposition 10.10.2)
.

Proposition 22
.

For any
d\in\mathbb{N}
,
p\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
there exists a bounded kernel
K:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
of order
p+1
with
\supp K\subseteq\mathcal{B}_{0}(1)
such that
s_{1}(K)
is invertible.

Proof.

If
p=0
, then the kernel
K
given by
K(\nu)\propto\max(1-\|\nu\|,0)
satisfies the requirements, since
s_{1}(K)=1
in that case. Henceforth assume that
p\in\mathbb{N}
. For
\epsilon>0
and
u\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, let
\phi_{\epsilon}(u):=\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{0}(\epsilon)}(u)/\epsilon^{d}
.
By Lemma
23
, there exists
\epsilon^{*}\in(0,1/2]
and
x_{1},\ldots,x_{\bar{p}^{*}}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(1/2)
such that

G:=\begin{pmatrix}g_{1}(x_{1})&\cdots&g_{1}(x_{\bar{p}^{*}})\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
g_{\bar{p}^{*}}(x_{1})&\cdots&g_{\bar{p}^{*}}(x_{\bar{p}^{*}})\end{pmatrix}

is invertible, where

g_{m}(x):=\int_{\mathcal{B}_{0}(\epsilon^{*})}Q_{m}^{\mathrm{ext}}(\nu)\phi_{\epsilon^{*}}(x-\nu)\,d\nu=\int_{\mathcal{B}_{0}(1)}Q_{m}^{\mathrm{ext}}(x+\epsilon^{*}\nu)\,d\nu

for
m\in[\bar{p}^{*}]
. Given an arbitrary vector
(c_{\alpha^{\prime}})_{\alpha^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}:p+1\leq\|\alpha^{\prime}\|_{1}\leq 2p}
and
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
with
\|\alpha\|_{1}\leq 2p
, define

\mu_{\alpha}:=\mathbbm{1}_{\{\alpha=\boldsymbol{0}\}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d},\\
p+1\leq\|\alpha^{\prime}\|_{1}\leq 2p\end{subarray}}\frac{c_{\alpha^{\prime}}}{\alpha^{\prime}!}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\alpha^{\prime}=\alpha\}}.

Now set
\mu:=(\mu_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d},\,\|\alpha\|_{1}\leq 2p}
, with components in graded lexicographical order. Further, let
d=(d_{1},\ldots,d_{\bar{p}^{*}})^{\top}:=G^{-1}\mu
and define a bounded function
K:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
with
\supp K\subseteq\mathcal{B}_{0}(1)
by

K(\nu):=\sum_{j=1}^{\bar{p}^{*}}d_{j}\phi_{\epsilon^{*}}(x_{j}-\nu).

For
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
with
\|\alpha\|_{1}\leq 2p
, let
m(\alpha)\in[\bar{p}^{*}]
denote the index for which
Q^{\mathrm{ext}}(\nu)_{m(\alpha)}=\nu^{\alpha}
. Then for any such
\alpha
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nu^{\alpha}K(\nu)\,d\nu

\displaystyle=\alpha!\mathrm{e}_{m(\alpha)}^{\top}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}K(\nu)Q^{\mathrm{ext}}(\nu)\,d\nu=\alpha!\mathrm{e}_{m(\alpha)}^{\top}\sum_{j=1}^{\bar{p}^{*}}d_{j}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{0}(\epsilon^{*})}\phi_{\epsilon^{*}}(x_{j}-\nu)Q^{\mathrm{ext}}(\nu)\,d\nu

\displaystyle=\alpha!\mathrm{e}_{m(\alpha)}^{\top}Gd=\alpha!\mu_{\alpha}=\begin{cases}1&\quad\text{if }\alpha=\boldsymbol{0}\\
0&\quad\text{if }1\leq\|\alpha\|_{1}\leq p\\
c_{\alpha}&\quad\text{if }p+1\leq\|\alpha\|_{1}\leq 2p\end{cases}.

(72)

Thus for arbitrary
(c_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d},p+1\leq\|\alpha\|_{1}\leq 2p}
there exists a bounded kernel
K
of order
p+1
with
\supp K\subseteq\mathcal{B}_{0}(1)
satisfying (
72
).

It remains to show we can choose
(c_{\alpha})
such that
s_{1}(K)
is invertible. To see this, we choose
(c_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d},p+1\leq\|\alpha\|_{1}\leq 2p}
to be a sequence of algebraically independent real numbers over
\mathbb{Q}
. Then
s_{1}(K)\in\mathbb{R}^{\bar{p}^{*}\times\bar{p}^{*}}
can be decomposed as

\displaystyle s_{1}(K)=\begin{pmatrix}1&{\boldsymbol{0}^{\top}}\\
\boldsymbol{0}&s_{1}^{(-)}(K)\end{pmatrix},\quad\text{where}\ s_{1}^{(-)}(K):=\begin{pmatrix}{\boldsymbol{0}}&{\boldsymbol{0}}&\cdots&{\boldsymbol{0}}&{\boldsymbol{0}}&S_{1,p}\\
{\boldsymbol{0}}&{\boldsymbol{0}}&\cdots&{\boldsymbol{0}}&S_{2,p-1}&S_{2,p}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots&\vdots\\
{\boldsymbol{0}}&{\boldsymbol{0}}&\cdots&S_{p-2,p-2}&S_{p-2,p-1}&S_{p-2,p}\\
{\boldsymbol{0}}&S_{p-1,2}&\cdots&S_{p-1,p-2}&S_{p-1,p-1}&S_{p-1,p}\\
S_{p,1}&S_{p,2}&\cdots&S_{p,p-2}&S_{p,p-1}&S_{p,p}\end{pmatrix},

and where

S_{k,\ell}:=(c_{\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}})_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}\,:\,\|\alpha_{1}\|_{1}=k,\|\alpha_{2}\|_{1}=\ell}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{C}^{k+d-1}_{d-1}\times\mathrm{C}^{\ell+d-1}_{d-1}},

with components in lexicographic order. Moreover,
S_{k,\ell}
consists of
\mathrm{C}^{k+\ell+d-1}_{d-1}
unique entries, such that each component of
(c_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}:\|\alpha\|_{1}=k+\ell}
appears in exactly one of the ‘antidiagonals’ of
S_{k,\ell}
.
Thus for any
k,\ell\in[p]
with
p+1\leq k+\ell\leq 2p
, we see from Lemma
24
that any
\min\bigl(\mathrm{C}^{k+d-1}_{d-1},\mathrm{C}^{\ell+d-1}_{d-1}\bigr)\times\min\bigl(\mathrm{C}^{k+d-1}_{d-1},\mathrm{C}^{\ell+d-1}_{d-1}\bigr)
submatrix of
S_{k,\ell}
is invertible, so

\mathrm{rank}(S_{k,\ell})=\min\bigl(\mathrm{C}^{k+d-1}_{d-1},\mathrm{C}^{\ell+d-1}_{d-1}\bigr).

(73)

If
p
is odd, then
S_{(p+1)/2,(p+1)/2}
is invertible by (
73
). If
p
is even, then we claim that

U:=\begin{pmatrix}{\boldsymbol{0}}&S_{p/2,p/2+1}\\
S_{p/2+1,p/2}&S_{p/2+1,p/2+1}\end{pmatrix},

is invertible. Indeed,
S_{p/2+1,p/2+1}
is invertible, and the Schur complement
of
S_{p/2+1,p/2+1}
with respect to
U
is

-S_{p/2+1,p/2}^{\top}S_{p/2+1,p/2+1}^{-1}S_{p/2+1,p/2}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{C}^{p/2+d-1}_{d-1}\times\mathrm{C}^{p/2+d-1}_{d-1}},

which is invertible as

\displaystyle\mathrm{rank}\bigl(S_{p/2+1,p/2}^{\top}S_{p/2+1,p/2+1}^{-1}S_{p/2+1,p/2}\bigr)=\mathrm{rank}\bigl(S_{p/2+1,p/2}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{C}^{p/2+d-1}_{d-1}.

We now apply Lemma
25
inductively. Suppose that

S_{[q,q_{0}]}:=\begin{pmatrix}{\boldsymbol{0}}&{\boldsymbol{0}}&\cdots&{\boldsymbol{0}}&{\boldsymbol{0}}&S_{q_{0},q}\\
{\boldsymbol{0}}&{\boldsymbol{0}}&\cdots&{\boldsymbol{0}}&S_{q_{0}+1,q-1}&S_{q_{0}+1,q}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots&\vdots\\
{\boldsymbol{0}}&{\boldsymbol{0}}&\cdots&S_{q-2,q-2}&S_{q-2,q-1}&S_{q-2,q}\\
{\boldsymbol{0}}&S_{q-1,q_{0}+1}&\cdots&S_{q-1,q-2}&S_{q-1,q-1}&S_{q-1,q}\\
S_{q,q_{0}}&S_{q,q_{0}+1}&\cdots&S_{q,q-2}&S_{q,q-1}&S_{q,q}\end{pmatrix},

is invertible for some
2\leq q_{0}\leq q\leq p-1
with
q+q_{0}-1=p
. Then by Lemma
25
with

\displaystyle A:=S_{[q,q_{0}]},\quad B:=S_{q+1,q_{0}-1},\quad D:=\begin{pmatrix}S_{q+1,q_{0}}&\cdots&S_{q+1,q}\end{pmatrix},

and
M_{\Phi}:\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{C}^{2q+d+3}_{d-1}}\to\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{C}^{q+d+1}_{d-1}\times\mathrm{C}^{q+d+1}_{d-1}}
of the form in Lemma
24
with
M_{\Phi}\bigl((c_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}:\|\alpha\|_{1}=2q+2}\bigr)=S_{q+1,q+1}
, we have that
S_{[q+1,q_{0}-1]}
is invertible. By induction, we conclude that
s_{1}^{(-)}(K)=S_{[p,1]}
is invertible, so
s_{1}(K)
is invertible.
∎

Lemma 23
.

There exist
\epsilon^{*}\in(0,1/2]
and
x_{1},\ldots,x_{\bar{p}^{*}}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(1/2)
such that the matrix
G\in\mathbb{R}^{\bar{p}^{*}\times\bar{p}^{*}}
with
(m,j)
th entry

G_{mj}:=\int_{\mathcal{B}_{0}(1)}Q^{\mathrm{ext}}_{m}(x_{j}+\epsilon^{*}u)\,du

is invertible.

Proof.

Since
Q^{\mathrm{ext}}_{1},\ldots,Q^{\mathrm{ext}}_{\bar{p}^{*}}
are linearly independent, by
76
, there exist
x_{1},\ldots,x_{\bar{p}^{*}}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(1/2)
such that

A:=\begin{pmatrix}Q^{\mathrm{ext}}_{1}(x_{1})&\cdots&Q^{\mathrm{ext}}_{1}(x_{\bar{p}^{*}})\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
Q^{\mathrm{ext}}_{\bar{p}^{*}}(x_{1})&\cdots&Q^{\mathrm{ext}}_{\bar{p}^{*}}(x_{\bar{p}^{*}})\end{pmatrix}

is invertible. Now define the continuous function
G:[0,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}^{\bar{p}^{*}\times\bar{p}^{*}}
by

G_{mj}(\epsilon):=\int_{\mathcal{B}_{0}(1)}Q^{\mathrm{ext}}_{m}(x_{j}+\epsilon u)\,du.

Then
\det G(0)=\det A\neq 0
, and
\det
is a continuous function, so there exists
\epsilon^{*}\in(0,1/2]
such that
\det G(\epsilon^{*})\neq 0
, as required.
∎

Lemma 24
.

Let
n\geq 2
, let
\Phi:[n]\to\mathcal{P}([m]\times[m])
be such that
\Phi(1),\ldots,\Phi(n)
partition
[m]\times[m]
, and such that for each
i\in[n]
, either
\Phi(i)\subseteq\{(j,m+1-j):j\in[m]\}
or
\Phi(i)\subseteq\{(j,m+1-k):j,k\in[m]\text{ are distinct}\}
. Now define
M_{\Phi}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{m\times m}
by
M_{\Phi}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})_{jk}:=\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{(j,k)\in\Phi(i)\}}
. Then for all
a\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
with components consisting of algebraically independent numbers over
\mathbb{Q}
, the matrix
M_{\Phi}(a)
is invertible.

Proof.

Define the determinant function
\mathcal{D}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}
by

\mathcal{D}(a):=\det M_{\Phi}(a).

Then
\mathcal{D}
is a polynomial with integer coefficients. Moreover,
\mathcal{D}\not\equiv 0
, because there exists
a^{*}\in\{0,1\}^{n}
such that
\mathcal{D}(a^{*})=\det\bigl((\mathbbm{1}_{\{j+k=m+1\}})_{j,k\in[m]}\bigr)=1
. Therefore, for arbitrary
a\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
whose components are algebraically independent numbers over
\mathbb{Q}
(thus also over
\mathbb{Z}
), we have
\mathcal{D}(a)\neq 0
, so
M_{\Phi}(a)
is invertible.
∎

Lemma 25
.

Let
m_{1}\leq m_{2}\leq m_{3}
and
n_{c}\geq 2
, and let
A\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{2}\times m_{2}}
,
B\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{3}\times m_{1}}
,
D\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{3}\times m_{2}}
, and
M:\mathbb{R}^{n_{c}}\to\mathbb{R}^{m_{3}\times m_{3}}
. Suppose that

(i)

A
is invertible;

(ii)

The matrix function
M
is of the form
M_{\Phi}
in Lemma
24
(with
m
replaced with
m_{3}
);

(iii)

B
has linearly independent columns.

Then for any
a\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{c}}
with algebraically independent components, the matrix

W:=\begin{pmatrix}{\boldsymbol{0}_{m_{1}\times m_{1}}}&{\boldsymbol{0}_{m_{1}\times m_{2}}}&B^{\top}\\
{\boldsymbol{0}_{m_{2}\times m_{1}}}&A&D^{\top}\\
B&D&M(a)\end{pmatrix}

is invertible.

Proof.

Take
a\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{c}}
. Since
A
is invertible, the matrix

W_{1}:=\begin{pmatrix}A&D^{\top}\\
D&M(a)\end{pmatrix}

is invertible if and only if the Schur complement of
A
, namely

W_{2}:=M(a)-DA^{-1}D^{\top},

is invertible. Now define the symmetric permutation matrix
\tilde{Q}=(\tilde{Q}_{ij})\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{3}\times m_{3}}
by
\tilde{Q}_{ij}:=\mathbbm{1}_{\{i+j=m_{3}+1\}}
. The determinant function

d(a):=\det\bigl(M(a)-DA^{-1}D^{\top}\bigr)

is a polynomial in
a
. Moreover, for any
\lambda>0
,
there exists
a_{\lambda}^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{c}}
such that
M(a_{\lambda}^{*})=\lambda\tilde{Q}
, and thus

d(a_{\lambda}^{*})=\det\bigl(\lambda\tilde{Q}-DA^{-1}D^{\top}\bigr)=\det\bigl(\lambda I_{m_{3}}-DA^{-1}D^{\top}\tilde{Q}\bigr).

It follows that
d\not\equiv 0
, since otherwise the matrix
DA^{-1}D^{\top}\tilde{Q}
has infinitely many eigenvalues.
Therefore for any
a\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{c}}
whose components are algebraically independent over
\mathbb{Q}
, we have
d(a)\neq 0
, so
W_{2}
and consequently
W_{1}
are both invertible.

The Schur complement of
W_{1}
with respect to
W
is then

W_{3}:=-\begin{pmatrix}{\boldsymbol{0}}_{m_{1}\times m_{2}}&B^{\top}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}A&D^{\top}\\
D&M(a)\end{pmatrix}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}{\boldsymbol{0}}_{m_{2}\times m_{1}}\\
B\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{1}\times m_{1}},

whose rank is equal to
\mathrm{rank}(B)=m_{1}
, so
W_{3}
is full rank, so invertible.
We conclude that the matrix
W
is invertible.
∎

Appendix C
Proof of Theorem
3

For
\omega:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
and
\beta>0
, define the
\beta
-Hölder seminorm

\|\omega\|_{C_{\beta}}:=\sup_{x\neq y}\frac{|\omega^{(\beta_{0})}(x)-\omega^{(\beta_{0})}(y)|}{\|x-y\|^{\beta-\beta_{0}}},

where
\beta_{0}:=\lceil\beta\rceil-1
.

Proof of Theorem
3
.

As shorthand, we write
\beta:=\beta
and
L:=L
.
For
x\in\mathcal{X}
, define
\sigma_{P}^{2}(x):=\Var_{P}(Y\,|\,X=x)
.
By Theorem
1
, for
\bar{f}\in\mathcal{H}(\beta,L)
,

\hat{f}_{n}^{\text{Outrig}}(x_{0})-\bar{f}(x_{0})=X_{n}^{\prime}+R_{n}^{\prime},\qquad X_{n}^{\prime}:=B(\bar{f},x_{0},K,h)h^{\beta^{*}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{nh^{d}}}\bigg\{\frac{R_{2}(K)V_{P,x_{0}}^{(\lambda)}(\rho)}{p_{X}(x_{0})}\bigg\}^{1/2}Z_{n}^{\prime}

while by more standard arguments,

\hat{f}_{n}^{\mathrm{LP}}(x_{0})-\bar{f}(x_{0})=X_{n}^{\prime\prime}+R_{n}^{\prime\prime},\qquad X_{n}^{\prime\prime}:=B(\bar{f},x_{0},K,h)h^{\beta^{*}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{nh^{d}}}\bigg\{\frac{R_{2}(K)\sigma_{P}^{2}(x_{0})}{p_{X}(x_{0})}\bigg\}^{1/2}Z_{n}^{\prime\prime},

where
Z_{n}^{\prime},Z_{n}^{\prime\prime},R_{n}^{\prime},R_{n}^{\prime\prime}
are random variables satisfying

\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\sup_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{X}}\sup_{(h,\lambda)\in\mathcal{H}_{n}\times\Lambda_{n}}\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\big|\mathbb{P}_{P}(Z_{n}^{\prime}\leq t)-\Phi(t)\big|\to 0,\;\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\sup_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{X}}\sup_{(h,\lambda)\in\mathcal{H}_{n}\times\Lambda_{n}}\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\big|\mathbb{P}_{P}(Z_{n}^{\prime\prime}\leq t)-\Phi(t)\big|\to 0,

as
n\to\infty
, where the supremum is taken over
P=(P_{X},P_{\varepsilon|X},\bar{f})
, and

R_{n}^{\prime},\,R_{n}^{\prime\prime}=o_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{X},\mathcal{H},\Lambda}\bigg(h^{\beta^{*}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{nh^{d}}}\bigg).

Take an arbitrary
\zeta>0
. Then using the fact that
(a+b)^{2}\leq(1+\zeta)(a^{2}+b^{2}/\zeta)
for
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
, we have on the event
\bigl\{|R_{n}^{\prime}|\leq\zeta s_{n,h}^{1/2}\bigr\}
that

\displaystyle\frac{(X_{n}^{\prime}+R_{n}^{\prime})^{2}}{s_{n,h}}\wedge M

\displaystyle\leq\biggl\{\frac{(1+\zeta)}{s_{n,h}}\Big(X_{n}^{\prime 2}+\frac{1}{\zeta}R_{n}^{\prime 2}\Big)\biggr\}\wedge M\leq\biggl\{\frac{(1+\zeta)X_{n}^{\prime 2}}{s_{n,h}}\wedge M\biggr\}+\frac{1+\zeta}{\zeta}\frac{R_{n}^{\prime 2}}{s_{n,h}}

\displaystyle\leq(1+\zeta)\Big(\frac{X_{n}^{\prime 2}}{s_{n,h}}\wedge M\Big)+\zeta(1+\zeta)\leq\biggl(\frac{X_{n}^{\prime 2}}{s_{n,h}}\wedge M\biggr)+\zeta M+\zeta(1+\zeta).

Thus

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl\{\bigl(s_{n,h}^{-1}(X_{n}^{\prime}+R_{n}^{\prime})^{2}\bigr)\wedge M\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\Bigl[\bigl\{\bigl(s_{n,h}^{-1}(X_{n}^{\prime}+R_{n}^{\prime})^{2}\bigr)\wedge M\bigr\}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|R_{n}^{\prime}|\leq\zeta s_{n,h}^{1/2}\}}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\mathbb{E}_{P}\Bigl[\bigl\{\bigl(s_{n,h}^{-1}(X_{n}^{\prime}+R_{n}^{\prime})^{2}\bigr)\wedge M\bigr\}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|R_{n}^{\prime}|>\zeta s_{n,h}^{1/2}\}}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggl(\frac{X_{n}^{\prime 2}}{s_{n,h}}\wedge M\biggr)+\zeta M+\zeta(1+\zeta)+M\,\mathbb{P}_{P}\bigl(|R_{n}^{\prime}|>\zeta s_{n,h}^{1/2}\bigr).

Analogously,

\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggl(\frac{X_{n}^{\prime 2}}{s_{n,h}}\wedge M\biggr)\leq\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl\{\bigl(s_{n,h}^{-1}(X_{n}^{\prime}+R_{n}^{\prime})^{2}\bigr)\wedge M\bigr\}+\zeta M+\zeta(1+\zeta)+M\,\mathbb{P}_{P}\bigl(|R_{n}^{\prime}|>\zeta s_{n,h}^{1/2}\bigr).

Therefore

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\sup_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{X}}

\displaystyle\sup_{(h,\lambda)\in\mathcal{H}_{n}\times\Lambda_{n}}\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl\{\bigl(s_{n,h}^{-1}(X_{n}^{\prime}+R_{n}^{\prime})^{2}\bigr)\wedge M\bigr\}-\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl\{\bigl(s_{n,h}^{-1}X_{n}^{\prime 2}\bigr)\wedge M\bigr\}\Bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\zeta M+\zeta(1+\zeta)+M\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\sup_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{X}}\sup_{(h,\lambda)\in\mathcal{H}_{n}\times\Lambda_{n}}\mathbb{P}_{P}(|s_{n,h}^{-1/2}R_{n}^{\prime}|>\zeta)

\displaystyle=\zeta M+\zeta(1+\zeta).

Since
\zeta>0
was arbitrary,

\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\sup_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{X}}\sup_{(h,\lambda)\in\mathcal{H}_{n}\times\Lambda_{n}}\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl\{\bigl(s_{n,h}^{-1}(X_{n}^{\prime}+R_{n}^{\prime})^{2}\bigr)\wedge M\bigr\}-\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl\{\bigl(s_{n,h}^{-1}X_{n}^{\prime 2}\bigr)\wedge M\bigr\}\Bigr|=0.

(74)

By almost identical arguments,

\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\sup_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{X}}\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}_{n}}\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl\{\bigl(s_{n,h}^{-1}(X_{n}^{\prime\prime}+R_{n}^{\prime\prime})^{2}\bigr)\wedge M\bigr\}-\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl\{\bigl(s_{n,h}^{-1}X_{n}^{\prime\prime 2}\bigr)\wedge M\bigr\}\Bigr|=0.

(75)

For
M>0
, define the function
G_{M}:\mathbb{R}\times[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)
by

G_{M}(\mu,V):=\mathbb{E}_{Z\sim N(\mu,V)}(Z^{2}\wedge M),

(76)

where we interpret
Z\sim N(\mu,0)
as a random variable satisfying
\mathbb{P}(Z=\mu)=1
. Further, for
\delta>0
as in Lemma
26
, define

C_{G}:=\inf_{t\in[0,1]}G_{M}\Bigl(\delta\sqrt{1-t},\,\frac{R_{2}(K)c_{3}t}{C_{X}}\Bigr).

(77)

Note in particular that
C_{G}
does not depend on
(P,x_{0},h,\lambda)
.
We also claim that
C_{G}>0
. Indeed,
C_{G}
is an infimum of a continuous function on a compact set, so the infimum is attained at some
t^{*}\in[0,1]
, and
C_{G}=G_{M}\big(\delta\sqrt{1-t^{*}},\,\tfrac{R_{2}(K)c_{3}t^{*}}{C_{X}}\big)>0
by Lemma
29
(iii)
. Now let
\eta\in\bigl(0,\frac{1}{4}C_{G}\bigr]
. By (
74
) and (
75
) there exists
N_{1}\in\mathbb{N}
, not depending on
(P,x_{0},h,\lambda)
, such that for
n\geq N_{1}
,

\frac{\mathcal{R}_{n,h,P,x_{0},\epsilon,M}(\hat{f}^{\mathrm{Outrig}})}{\mathcal{R}_{n,h,P,x_{0},\epsilon,M}(\hat{f}^{\mathrm{LP}})}\leq\frac{\sup_{\tilde{f}\in\mathcal{H}_{f,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\beta,L)}\mathbb{E}_{(P_{X},P_{\varepsilon|X},\tilde{f})}\bigl\{\big(s_{n,h}^{-1}X_{n}^{\prime 2}\big)\wedge M\bigr\}+\eta}{\sup_{\tilde{f}\in\mathcal{H}_{f,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\beta,L)}\mathbb{E}_{(P_{X},P_{\varepsilon|X},\tilde{f})}\bigl\{\big(s_{n,h}^{-1}X_{n}^{\prime\prime 2}\big)\wedge M\bigr\}-\eta}.

Now take
Y\sim N(0,1)
and let

\displaystyle Y_{n}^{\prime}\equiv Y_{n}^{\prime}(\bar{f})

\displaystyle:=B(\bar{f},x_{0},K,h)h^{\beta^{*}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{nh^{d}}}\biggl(\frac{R_{2}(K)V_{P,x_{0}}^{(\lambda)}(\rho)}{p_{X}(x_{0})}\biggr)^{1/2}Y,

\displaystyle Y_{n}^{\prime\prime}\equiv Y_{n}^{\prime\prime}(\bar{f})

\displaystyle:=B(\bar{f},x_{0},K,h)h^{\beta^{*}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{nh^{d}}}\biggl(\frac{R_{2}(K)\sigma_{P}^{2}(x_{0})}{p_{X}(x_{0})}\biggr)^{1/2}Y.

By Lemma
27
, there exists
N_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
, not depending on
(P,x_{0},h,\lambda)
, such that for all
n\geq N_{1}\vee N_{2}
,

\frac{\mathcal{R}_{n,h,P,x_{0},\epsilon,M}(\hat{f}^{\mathrm{Outrig}})}{\mathcal{R}_{n,h,P,x_{0},\epsilon,M}(\hat{f}^{\mathrm{LP}})}\leq\frac{\sup_{\tilde{f}\in\mathcal{H}_{f,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\beta,L)}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl\{s_{n,h}^{-1}\bigl(Y_{n}^{\prime}(\tilde{f})\bigr)^{2}\bigr\}\wedge M\bigr]+2\eta}{\sup_{\tilde{f}\in\mathcal{H}_{f,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\beta,L)}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl\{s_{n,h}^{-1}\bigl(Y_{n}^{\prime}(\tilde{f})\bigr)^{2}\bigr\}\wedge M\bigr]-2\eta}.

From hereon we will take
n\geq N_{1}\vee N_{2}
.
Noting that
G_{M}(\mu,V)=G_{M}(-\mu,V)
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl\{s_{n,h}^{-1}\bigl(Y_{n}^{\prime}(\tilde{f})\bigr)^{2}\bigr\}\wedge M\bigr]

\displaystyle=G_{M}\bigg(|B(\tilde{f},x_{0},K,h)|s_{n,h}^{-1/2}h^{\beta^{*}},\,\frac{R_{2}(K)V_{P,x_{0}}^{(\lambda)}(\rho)}{p_{X}(x_{0})}\frac{1}{s_{n,h}nh^{d}}\bigg),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl\{s_{n,h}^{-1}\bigl(Y_{n}^{\prime}(\tilde{f})\bigr)^{2}\bigr\}\wedge M\bigr]

\displaystyle=G_{M}\bigg(|B(\tilde{f},x_{0},K,h)|s_{n,h}^{-1/2}h^{\beta^{*}},\,\frac{R_{2}(K)\sigma_{P}^{2}(x_{0})}{p_{X}(x_{0})}\frac{1}{s_{n,h}nh^{d}}\bigg).

By Lemma
29
(i)
,
\mu\mapsto G_{M}(\mu,V)
is strictly increasing in
|\mu|
, for each
M,V>0
. Therefore, for any
V>0
,

\sup_{\tilde{f}\in\mathcal{H}_{f,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\beta,L)}G_{M}\bigl(|B(\tilde{f},x_{0},K,h)|s_{n,h}^{-1/2}h^{\beta^{*}},\,V\bigr)=G_{M}\bigl(\bar{B}_{f,\epsilon,x_{0},K,h}\,s_{n,h}^{-1/2}h^{\beta^{*}},\,V\bigr),

where we use the shorthand

\bar{B}_{f,\epsilon,x_{0},K,h}:=\sup_{\tilde{f}\in\mathcal{H}_{f,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\beta,L)}|B(\tilde{f},x_{0},K,h)|.

Therefore

\frac{\sup_{\tilde{f}\in\mathcal{H}_{f,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\beta,L)}\mathbb{E}\big[\big(s_{n,h}^{-1}\{Y_{n}^{\prime}(\tilde{f})\}^{2}\big)\wedge M\big]+2\eta}{\sup_{\tilde{f}\in\mathcal{H}_{f,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\beta,L)}\mathbb{E}\big[\big(s_{n,h}^{-1}\{Y_{n}^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{f})\}^{2}\big)\wedge M\big]-2\eta}=\frac{G_{M}\Big(\bar{B}_{f,\epsilon,x_{0},K,h}\,s_{n,h}^{-1/2}h^{\beta^{*}},\,\frac{R_{2}(K)V_{P,x_{0}}^{(\lambda)}(\rho)}{p_{X}(x_{0})}\frac{1}{s_{n,h}nh^{d}}\Big)+2\eta}{G_{M}\Big(\bar{B}_{f,\epsilon,x_{0},K,h}\,s_{n,h}^{-1/2}h^{\beta^{*}},\,\frac{R_{2}(K)\sigma_{P}^{2}(x_{0})}{p_{X}(x_{0})}\frac{1}{s_{n,h}nh^{d}}\Big)-2\eta},

and so

\displaystyle\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}

\displaystyle\sup_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{X}}\sup_{(h,\lambda)\in\mathcal{H}_{n}\times\Lambda_{n}}\frac{\mathcal{R}_{n,h,P,x_{0},\epsilon,M}(\hat{f}^{\mathrm{Outrig}})}{\mathcal{R}_{n,h,P,x_{0},\epsilon,M}(\hat{f}^{\mathrm{LP}})}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\sup_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{X}}\sup_{(h,\lambda)\in\mathcal{H}_{n}\times\Lambda_{n}}\frac{G_{M}\Big(\bar{B}_{f,\epsilon,x_{0},K,h}\,s_{n,h}^{-1/2}h^{\beta^{*}},\,\frac{R_{2}(K)V_{P,x_{0}}^{(\lambda)}(\rho)}{p_{X}(x_{0})}\frac{1}{s_{n,h}nh^{d}}\Big)+2\eta}{G_{M}\Big(\bar{B}_{f,\epsilon,x_{0},K,h}\,s_{n,h}^{-1/2}h^{\beta^{*}},\,\frac{R_{2}(K)\sigma_{P}^{2}(x_{0})}{p_{X}(x_{0})}\frac{1}{s_{n,h}nh^{d}}\Big)-2\eta}

\displaystyle=\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\sup_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{X}}\sup_{(h,\lambda)\in\mathcal{H}_{n}\times\Lambda_{n}}\frac{G_{M}\Big(\bar{B}_{f,\epsilon,x_{0},K,h}\,\alpha_{1}\big(nh^{2\beta^{*}\hskip-0.81949pt+d}\big),\,\frac{R_{2}(K)V_{P,x_{0}}^{(\lambda)}(\rho)}{p_{X}(x_{0})}\alpha_{2}\big(nh^{2\beta^{*}\hskip-0.81949pt+d}\big)\Big)+2\eta}{G_{M}\Big(\bar{B}_{f,\epsilon,x_{0},K,h}\,\alpha_{1}\big(nh^{2\beta^{*}\hskip-0.81949pt+d}\big),\,\frac{R_{2}(K)\sigma_{P}^{2}(x_{0})}{p_{X}(x_{0})}\alpha_{2}\big(nh^{2\beta^{*}\hskip-0.81949pt+d}\big)\Big)-2\eta},

(78)

where
\alpha_{1}(u):=\sqrt{\frac{u}{1+u}}
and
\alpha_{2}(u):=\frac{1}{1+u}
. By Lemma
15
(ii)
there exists
C_{B}(K,d,\beta_{0}^{*},L)>0
, not depending on
(P,x_{0},h,\lambda)
, such that

|B(\tilde{f},x_{0},K,h)|\alpha_{1}\big(nh^{2\beta^{*}\hskip-0.81949pt+d}\big)\leq C_{B}(K,d,\beta_{0}^{*},L).

By Lemma
31
,
V_{P,x_{0}}^{(\lambda)}(\rho_{\mathrm{score}})\leq\sigma_{P}^{2}(x_{0})
, and moreover, by Lemma
29
,
G_{M}
is coordinate-wise strictly increasing on the restricted domain
[0,C_{B}(K,d,\beta_{0}^{*},L)]\times[0,\infty)
for
M\geq\frac{25}{4}C_{B}(K,d,\beta_{0}^{*},L)^{2}
. Hence, for such
M
,

\displaystyle\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}

\displaystyle\sup_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{X}}\sup_{(h,\lambda)\in\mathcal{H}_{n}\times\Lambda_{n}}\frac{G_{M}\Big(\bar{B}_{f,\epsilon,x_{0},K,h}\,\alpha_{1}\big(nh^{2\beta^{*}\hskip-0.81949pt+d}\big),\,\frac{R_{2}(K)V_{P,x_{0}}^{(\lambda)}(\rho_{\mathrm{score}})}{p_{X}(x_{0})}\alpha_{2}\big(nh^{2\beta^{*}\hskip-0.81949pt+d}\big)\Big)+2\eta}{G_{M}\Big(\bar{B}_{f,\epsilon,x_{0},K,h}\,\alpha_{1}\big(nh^{2\beta^{*}\hskip-0.81949pt+d}\big),\,\frac{R_{2}(K)\sigma_{P}^{2}(x_{0})}{p_{X}(x_{0})}\alpha_{2}\big(nh^{2\beta^{*}\hskip-0.81949pt+d}\big)\Big)-2\eta}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\sup_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{X}}\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}_{n}}\frac{G_{M}\Big(\bar{B}_{f,\epsilon,x_{0},K,h}\,\alpha_{1}\big(nh^{2\beta^{*}\hskip-0.81949pt+d}\big),\,\frac{R_{2}(K)\sigma_{P}^{2}(x_{0})}{p_{X}(x_{0})}\alpha_{2}\big(nh^{2\beta^{*}\hskip-0.81949pt+d}\big)\Big)+2\eta}{G_{M}\Big(\bar{B}_{f,\epsilon,x_{0},K,h}\,\alpha_{1}\big(nh^{2\beta^{*}\hskip-0.81949pt+d}\big),\,\frac{R_{2}(K)\sigma_{P}^{2}(x_{0})}{p_{X}(x_{0})}\alpha_{2}\big(nh^{2\beta^{*}\hskip-0.81949pt+d}\big)\Big)-2\eta}

\displaystyle=1+\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\sup_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{X}}\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}_{n}}\frac{4\eta}{G_{M}\Big(\bar{B}_{f,\epsilon,x_{0},K,h}\,\alpha_{1}\big(nh^{2\beta^{*}\hskip-0.81949pt+d}\big),\,\frac{R_{2}(K)\sigma_{P}^{2}(x_{0})}{p_{X}(x_{0})}\alpha_{2}\big(nh^{2\beta^{*}\hskip-0.81949pt+d}\big)\Big)-2\eta}.

(79)

Now, by Lemma
26
, there exists
N_{3}\in\mathbb{N}
and
\delta>0
, neither depending on
(P,x_{0},h,\lambda)
, such that for all
n\geq N_{3}
,

\inf_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\inf_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{X}}\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}_{n}}\bar{B}_{f,\epsilon,x_{0},K,h}\,=\inf_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\inf_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{X}}\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}_{n}}\sup_{\tilde{f}\in\mathcal{H}_{f,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\beta,L)}|B(\tilde{f},x_{0},K,h)|\geq\delta.

From hereon we take
n\geq N_{1}\vee N_{2}\vee N_{3}
.
Then for
M\geq\frac{25}{4}C_{B}(K,d,\beta_{0}^{*},L)^{2}
,

\displaystyle\inf_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\inf_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{X}}\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}_{n}}

\displaystyle G_{M}\bigg(\bar{B}_{f,\epsilon,x_{0},K,h}\,\alpha_{1}\big(nh^{2\beta^{*}\hskip-0.81949pt+d}\big),\,\frac{R_{2}(K)\sigma_{P}^{2}(x_{0})}{p_{X}(x_{0})}\alpha_{2}\big(nh^{2\beta^{*}\hskip-0.81949pt+d}\big)\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}_{n}}G_{M}\bigg(\delta\alpha_{1}\big(nh^{2\beta^{*}\hskip-0.81949pt+d}\big),\,\frac{R_{2}(K)c_{3}}{C_{X}}\alpha_{2}\big(nh^{2\beta^{*}\hskip-0.81949pt+d}\big)\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{t\in[0,1]}G_{M}\biggl(\delta\sqrt{1-t},\,\frac{R_{2}(K)c_{3}t}{C_{X}}\biggr)=C_{G},

(80)

with the final inequality following from the facts that
\alpha_{1}=\sqrt{1-\alpha_{2}}
, and
\alpha_{2}([0,\infty])=[0,1]
. Thus for
\eta\in(0,\frac{1}{4}C_{G}]
,

\displaystyle\quad\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\sup_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{X}}\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}_{n}}\frac{4\eta}{G_{M}\Big(\bar{B}_{f,\epsilon,x_{0},K,h}\,\alpha_{1}\big(nh^{2\beta^{*}\hskip-0.81949pt+d}\big),\,\frac{R_{2}(K)\sigma_{P}^{2}(x_{0})}{p_{X}(x_{0})}\alpha_{2}\big(nh^{2\beta^{*}\hskip-0.81949pt+d}\big)\Big)-2\eta}\leq\frac{8\eta}{C_{G}}.

(81)

Combining (
78
), (
79
) and (
81
),

\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\sup_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{X}}\sup_{(h,\lambda)\in\mathcal{H}_{n}\times\Lambda_{n}}\frac{\mathcal{R}_{n,h,P,x_{0},\epsilon,M}(\hat{f}^{\mathrm{Outrig}})}{\mathcal{R}_{n,h,P,x_{0},\epsilon,M}(\hat{f}^{\mathrm{LP}})}\leq 1+\frac{8\eta}{C_{G}}.

Since
\eta\in\big(0,\frac{1}{4}C_{G}\big]
,
M\geq\frac{25}{4}C_{B}(K,d,\beta_{0}^{*},L)^{2}
and
\epsilon>0
were arbitrary, the result follows.
∎

C.1
Auxiliary results for Theorem
3

Lemma 26
.

Let
\beta,L,\epsilon>0
and suppose that the kernel
K
satisfies Assumption
(A2.1)
. Then there exists
\delta\equiv\delta(\beta,L,\epsilon,K)>0
such that

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\inf_{f\in\mathcal{H}(\beta,L)}\inf_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{X}}\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}_{n}}\sup_{\tilde{f}\in\mathcal{H}_{f,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\beta,L)}|B(\tilde{f},x_{0},K,h)|\geq\delta,

(82)

where
B
is as in (
40
).

Proof.

Define
\psi:\mathbb{R}\to[0,1]
and
\omega:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
by

\psi(u):=\begin{cases}1&\quad\text{if }|u|<1\\
\exp\Big(1-\frac{1}{|u|(2-|u|)}\Big)&\quad\text{if }1\leq|u|\leq 2\\
0&\quad\text{if }|u|>2\end{cases},\qquad\omega(u):=|u|^{\beta^{*}}\psi(u).

(83)

Then
\|\omega\|_{C_{\beta^{*}}}<\infty
by Lemma
28
.
Further define

a_{\beta^{*}}:=\begin{cases}1&\quad\text{if $\beta_{0}^{*}=0$}\\
\frac{(\beta^{*})_{\beta_{0}^{*}-1}}{(\beta_{0}^{*}-1)!}&\quad\text{if $\beta_{0}^{*}\geq 1$}\end{cases}.

We will prove (
82
) with
\delta:=\frac{\xi}{2-\xi}\frac{a_{\beta^{*}}L}{d\|\omega\|_{C_{\beta^{*}}}}|\mu_{\beta^{*}}(K)|
, where
\xi:=\frac{2\epsilon}{2L+\epsilon}\wedge 1
.
Take an arbitrary
x_{0}\in\mathcal{X}
. If
|B(f,x_{0},K,h)|\geq\delta
then (
82
) follows immediately. We claim that if
|B(f,x_{0},K,h)|<\delta
then there exists a sequence
\tilde{f}_{n}\in\mathcal{H}(\beta,L)
satisfying

\|\tilde{f}_{n}-f\|_{\infty}\leq\epsilon\quad\text{and}\quad\inf_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\inf_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{X}}\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}_{n}}|B(\tilde{f}_{n},x_{0},K,h)|\geq\delta

for sufficiently large
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
Define
g:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
by

g(x;h):=\frac{Lh^{\beta^{*}}}{d\|\omega\|_{C_{\beta^{*}}}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\omega\bigg(\frac{(x-x_{0})_{\ell}}{h}\bigg).

Note the following properties of
g
:

(i)

\|g(\cdot\,;h)\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{L\|\omega\|_{\infty}}{\|\omega\|_{C_{\beta^{*}}}}\,h^{\beta^{*}}
.

(ii)

For all
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
with
\|\alpha\|_{1}\leq\beta^{*}_{0}
we have
\|\partial^{\alpha}g(\cdot\,;h)\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{L\|\partial^{\alpha}\omega\|_{\infty}}{d\|\omega\|_{C_{\beta^{*}}}}h^{\beta^{*}-\|\alpha\|_{1}}
.

(iii)

Take
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
with
\|\alpha\|_{1}=\beta_{0}^{*}
.
In the case
\beta_{0}^{*}=0
,

\frac{|g(x;h)-g(y;h)|}{\|x-y\|^{\beta^{*}}}\leq L.

Now consider the case
\beta_{0}^{*}\geq 1
.
If
\|\alpha\|_{0}\geq 2
then
\partial^{\alpha}g(x;h)=0
.
If
\|\alpha\|_{0}=1
, then define
\ell^{*}\in[d]
to be the index with
\alpha_{\ell^{*}}=\beta_{0}^{*}
. Then

\frac{|\partial^{\alpha}g(x;h)-\partial^{\alpha}g(y;h)|}{\|x-y\|^{\beta^{*}-\beta_{0}^{*}}}=\frac{\Big|\frac{\partial^{\beta_{0}^{*}}}{\partial x_{\ell^{*}}^{\beta_{0}^{*}}}g(x;h)-\frac{\partial^{\beta_{0}^{*}}}{\partial x_{\ell^{*}}^{\beta_{0}^{*}}}g(y;h)\Big|}{\|x-y\|^{\beta^{*}-\beta_{0}^{*}}}\\
=\frac{Lh^{\beta^{*}-\beta_{0}^{*}}}{d\|\omega\|_{C_{\beta^{*}}}}\cdot\frac{\bigl|\omega^{(\beta_{0})}\bigl(\frac{(x-x_{0})_{\ell^{*}}}{h}\bigr)-\omega^{(\beta_{0})}\bigl(\frac{\{y-x_{0}\}_{\ell^{*}}}{h}\bigr)\bigr|}{\|x-y\|^{\beta^{*}-\beta_{0}^{*}}}\leq\frac{L}{d}\cdot\bigg(\frac{|(x-x_{0})_{\ell^{*}}|}{\|x-y\|}\bigg)^{\beta^{*}-\beta_{0}^{*}}\leq L.

(iv)

Take
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
with
\|\alpha\|_{1}=\beta_{0}^{*}
.
Consider first the case
\beta_{0}^{*}\geq 1
.
If
\|\alpha\|_{0}\geq 2
, then

\int_{\mathcal{B}_{0}(1)}K(\nu)\nu^{\alpha}\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{\beta_{0}^{*}-1}\bigl\{\partial^{\alpha}g(x_{0}+th\nu;h)-\partial^{\alpha}g(x_{0};h)\bigr\}\,dt\,d\nu=0.

Now consider the case
\|\alpha\|_{0}=1
, and let
\ell^{*}\in[d]
be the index with
\alpha_{\ell^{*}}=\beta_{0}^{*}
. For
\nu\in\supp K
we have
\|\nu\|\leq 1
, and so
|t\nu_{\ell^{*}}|\leq 1
for all
t\in[0,1]
and
\psi\big(\frac{\{(x_{0}+th\nu)-x_{0}\}_{\ell^{*}}}{h}\big)=\psi(t\nu_{\ell^{*}})=1
. Hence

g(x;h)=\frac{L}{d\|\omega\|_{C_{\beta^{*}}}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}|(x-x_{0})_{\ell}|^{\beta^{*}}\psi\bigg(\frac{(x-x_{0})_{\ell}}{h}\bigg)=\frac{L}{d\|\omega\|_{C_{\beta^{*}}}}\|x-x_{0}\|_{\beta^{*}}^{\beta^{*}}

(84)

for
x\in\bigl\{x_{0}+th\nu\,:\,(\nu,t)\in(\supp K)\times[0,1]\bigr\}
. It follows that

\partial^{\alpha}g(x;h)=\frac{L}{d\|\omega\|_{C_{\beta^{*}}}}(\beta^{*})_{\beta_{0}^{*}}|(x-x_{0})_{\ell^{*}}|^{\beta^{*}-\beta_{0}^{*}}\sgn^{\beta_{0}^{*}}\bigl((x-x_{0})_{\ell^{*}})\bigr),

again for
x\in\{x_{0}+th\nu\,:\,(\nu,t)\in(\supp K)\times[0,1]\}
. In particular, for
\nu\in\supp K
and
t\in[0,1]
,

\partial^{\alpha}g(x_{0}+th\nu;h)=\frac{Lt^{\beta^{*}-\beta_{0}^{*}}h^{\beta^{*}-\beta_{0}^{*}}}{d\|\omega\|_{C_{\beta^{*}}}}(\beta^{*})_{\beta_{0}^{*}}|\nu_{\ell^{*}}|^{\beta^{*}-\beta_{0}^{*}}\sgn^{\beta_{0}^{*}}(\nu_{\ell^{*}}).

We deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{B}_{0}(1)}K(\nu)\nu^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{\beta_{0}^{*}-1}\bigl\{\partial^{\alpha}g(x_{0}+th\nu)-\partial^{\alpha}g(x_{0})\bigr\}\,dt\,d\nu

\displaystyle=\frac{L(\beta^{*})_{\beta_{0}^{*}}}{d\|\omega\|_{C_{\beta^{*}}}}h^{\beta^{*}-\beta_{0}^{*}}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{0}(1)}K(\nu)|\nu_{\ell^{*}}|^{\beta^{*}}\int_{0}^{1}t^{\beta^{*}-\beta_{0}^{*}}dt\,d\nu

\displaystyle=\frac{L(\beta^{*})_{\beta^{*}_{0}-1}}{d\|\omega\|_{C_{\beta^{*}}}}h^{\beta^{*}-\beta_{0}^{*}}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{0}(1)}K(\nu)|\nu_{\ell^{*}}|^{\beta^{*}}\,d\nu.

Thus

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle\bigl(g(\cdot\,;h),x_{0},K,h\bigr)

\displaystyle=h^{\beta_{0}^{*}-\beta^{*}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}:\\
\|\alpha\|_{1}=\beta_{0}^{*},\|\alpha\|_{0}=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{0}^{*}}{\alpha!}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{0}(1)}K(\nu)\nu^{\alpha}\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{\beta_{0}^{*}-1}\bigl\{\partial^{\alpha}g(x_{0}+th\nu;h)-\partial^{\alpha}g(x_{0};h)\bigr\}\,dt\,d\nu

\displaystyle=\frac{L(\beta^{*})_{\beta^{*}_{0}-1}}{d\|\omega\|_{C_{\beta^{*}}}(\beta_{0}^{*}-1)!}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{0}(1)}K(\nu)|\nu_{\ell}|^{\beta^{*}}\,d\nu=\frac{L(\beta^{*})_{\beta^{*}_{0}-1}}{d\|\omega\|_{C_{\beta^{*}}}(\beta_{0}^{*}-1)!}\mu_{\beta^{*}}(K).

On the other hand, if
\beta_{0}^{*}=0
, then

B\bigl(g(\cdot\,;h),x_{0},K,h\bigr)=h^{-\beta^{*}}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{0}(1)}K(\nu)\bigl\{g(x_{0}+h\nu;h)-g(x_{0};h)\bigr\}\,d\nu=\frac{L}{d\|\omega\|_{C_{\beta^{*}}}}\mu_{\beta^{*}}(K).

We conclude that

B\bigl(g(\cdot\,;h),x_{0},K,h\bigr)=\frac{a_{\beta^{*}}L}{d\|\omega\|_{C_{\beta^{*}}}}\mu_{\beta^{*}}(K).

Now, for
n\in\mathbb{N}
, define

h_{n}:=a_{n}\vee\biggl(\frac{\|\omega\|_{C_{\beta^{*}}}}{\|\omega\|_{\infty}}\biggr)^{1/\beta^{*}}\wedge b_{n},

and

\tilde{f}_{n}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
by

\tilde{f}_{n}(x):=(1-\xi)f(x)+\xi\,g(x;h_{n}).

Take
N\in\mathbb{N}
large enough that

b_{n}\leq\biggl(\frac{\|\omega\|_{C_{\beta^{*}}}}{\|\omega\|_{\infty}}\biggr)^{1/\beta^{*}}\wedge\biggl(\frac{d\|\omega\|_{C_{\beta^{*}}}}{\sup_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}:\|\alpha\|_{1}\leq\beta_{0}^{*}}\|\partial^{\alpha}\omega\|_{\infty}}\biggr)^{1/(\beta^{*}-\beta_{0}^{*})}\wedge\biggl(\frac{\epsilon\|\omega\|_{C_{\beta^{*}}}}{2L\|\omega\|_{\infty}}\biggr)^{1/\beta^{*}}\wedge 1

for all
n\geq N
. Then, for such
n
,

\|\tilde{f}_{n}\|_{\infty}\leq(1-\xi)\|f\|_{\infty}+\xi\|g(\cdot\,;h_{n})\|_{\infty}\leq(1-\xi)L+\xi\frac{L\|\omega\|_{\infty}h_{n}^{\beta^{*}}}{\|\omega\|_{C_{\beta^{*}}}}\leq L.

Moreover, for
\beta_{0}^{*}\geq 1
and
n\geq N
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}:\|\alpha\|_{1}\leq\beta^{*}_{0}}\|\partial^{\alpha}\tilde{f}_{n}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}:\|\alpha\|_{1}\leq\beta^{*}_{0}}\Bigl\{(1-\xi)\|\partial^{\alpha}f\|_{\infty}+\xi\|\partial^{\alpha}g(\cdot\,;h_{n})\|_{\infty}\Bigr\}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\xi)L+\xi Lh^{\beta^{*}-\beta^{*}_{0}}\frac{\sup_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}:\|\alpha\|_{1}\leq\beta_{0}^{*}}\|\partial^{\alpha}\omega\|_{\infty}}{d\|\omega\|_{C_{\beta^{*}}}}\leq L.

Finally, for all
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
with
\|\alpha\|_{1}=\beta_{0}^{*}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{x\neq y}\frac{|\partial^{\alpha}\tilde{f}_{n}(x)-\partial^{\alpha}\tilde{f}_{n}(y)|}{\|x-y\|^{\beta^{*}-\beta_{0}^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\xi)\sup_{x\neq y}\frac{|\partial^{\alpha}f(x)-\partial^{\alpha}f(y)|}{\|x-y\|^{\beta^{*}-\beta_{0}^{*}}}+\xi\sup_{x\neq y}\frac{|\partial^{\alpha}g(x;h_{n})-\partial^{\alpha}g(y;h_{n})|}{\|x-y\|^{\beta^{*}-\beta_{0}^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\xi)L+\xi L=L.

Hence
\tilde{f}_{n}\in\mathcal{H}(\beta,L)
for
n\geq N
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\|\tilde{f}_{n}-f\|_{\infty}\leq\xi(\|f\|_{\infty}+\|g(\cdot\,;h_{n})\|_{\infty})\leq\xi(L+\epsilon/2)=\epsilon,

for all
n\geq N
, so
\tilde{f}_{n}\in\mathcal{H}_{f,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\beta,L)
for such
n
. Finally, by the linearity of the bias function (
40
) in its first argument,

B(\tilde{f}_{n},x_{0},K,h_{n})=(1-\xi)B(f,x_{0},K,h_{n})+\xi B\bigl(g(\cdot\,;h_{n}),x_{0},K,h_{n}\bigr),

and so

\displaystyle\bigl|B(\tilde{f}_{n},x_{0},K,h_{n})\bigr|

\displaystyle\geq\xi\bigl|B(g(\cdot\,;h_{n}),x_{0},K,h_{n})\bigr|-(1-\xi)|B(f,x_{0},K,h_{n})|

\displaystyle>\frac{\xi a_{\beta^{*}}L}{d\|\omega\|_{C_{\beta^{*}}}}|\mu_{\beta^{*}}(K)|-(1-\xi)\delta=\delta,

as required.
∎

Lemma 27
.

Consider a sequence of random variables
(Z_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
that depend on parameters
P\in\mathcal{P}
,
x_{0}\in\mathcal{X}
,
h\in\mathcal{H}_{n}
,
\lambda\in\Lambda_{n}
with

\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\sup_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{X}}\sup_{(h,\lambda)\in\mathcal{H}_{n}\times\Lambda_{n}}\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\big|\mathbb{P}_{P}(Z_{n}\leq t)-\Phi(t)\big|\rightarrow 0.

Given a real sequence
(\mu_{n})
and a positive sequence
(\sigma_{n})
, define
X_{n}:=\mu_{n}+\sigma_{n}Z_{n}
and
\tilde{X}_{n}:=\mu_{n}+\sigma_{n}Z
, where
Z\sim N(0,1)
. For the loss function
\ell
given by
\ell(u):=u^{2}\wedge M
for some
M>0
, we have

\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\sup_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{X}}\sup_{(h,\lambda)\in\mathcal{H}_{n}\times\Lambda_{n}}\bigl|\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl(\ell(X_{n})\bigr)-\mathbb{E}\bigl(\ell(\tilde{X}_{n})\bigr)\bigr|\to 0.

Proof.

As
\ell
takes values in
[0,M]
, we have

\displaystyle\bigl|\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl(\ell(X_{n})\bigr)

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\bigl(\ell(\tilde{X}_{n})\bigr)\bigr|=\biggl|\int_{0}^{M}\bigl\{\mathbb{P}_{P}\bigl(\ell(X_{n})>t\bigr)-\mathbb{P}\bigl(\ell(\tilde{X}_{n})>t\bigr)\bigr\}\,dt\biggr|

\displaystyle\leq M\sup_{t\in[0,M]}\bigl|\mathbb{P}_{P}\bigl(\ell(X_{n})>t\bigr)-\mathbb{P}\bigl(\ell(\tilde{X}_{n})>t\bigr)\bigr|

\displaystyle=M\sup_{t\in[0,M]}\biggl|\mathbb{P}_{P}\biggl(\frac{-\sqrt{t}-\mu_{n}}{\sigma_{n}}\leq Z_{n}\leq\frac{\sqrt{t}-\mu_{n}}{\sigma_{n}}\biggr)-\mathbb{P}\biggl(\frac{-\sqrt{t}-\mu_{n}}{\sigma_{n}}\leq Z\leq\frac{\sqrt{t}-\mu_{n}}{\sigma_{n}}\biggr)\biggr|

\displaystyle\leq M\sup_{t\in[0,M]}\biggl|\mathbb{P}_{P}\biggl(Z_{n}\leq\frac{\sqrt{t}-\mu_{n}}{\sigma_{n}}\biggr)-\mathbb{P}\biggl(Z\leq\frac{\sqrt{t}-\mu_{n}}{\sigma_{n}}\biggr)\biggr|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+M\sup_{t\in[0,M]}\biggl|\mathbb{P}_{P}\biggl(Z_{n}<\frac{-\sqrt{t}-\mu_{n}}{\sigma_{n}}\biggr)-\mathbb{P}\biggl(Z<\frac{-\sqrt{t}-\mu_{n}}{\sigma_{n}}\biggr)\biggr|.

Taking suprema and the limit as
n\to\infty
yields the required result.
∎

Lemma 28
.

Adopt the notation of Lemma
26
. The function
\omega
of the form (
83
) has finite
\beta^{*}
-Hölder seminorm.

Proof.

Since
\psi
is infinitely differentiable, with bounded derivatives of all orders, for any
j\in\mathbb{N}
and
\beta_{\psi}\in(0,1]
, there exists
L_{j,\beta_{\psi}}\in(0,\infty)
such that
\psi^{(j)}\in\mathcal{H}(\beta_{\psi},L_{j,\beta_{\psi}})
. Define

C_{\psi}:=\max_{j\in\{0,1,\ldots,\beta_{0}^{*}\}}\sup_{u\in\mathbb{R}}\bigl|\psi^{(j)}(u)\bigr|<\infty.

By the Leibniz rule,

\omega^{(\beta_{0}^{*})}(u)=\sum_{j=0}^{\beta_{0}^{*}}{\binom{\beta_{0}^{*}}{j}}(\beta^{*})_{j}|u|^{\beta^{*}-j}(\sgn u)^{j}\psi^{(\beta_{0}^{*}-j)}(u),

where
(x)_{j}:=\prod_{k=1}^{j}(x+1-k)
denotes the
j
th falling factorial.
We consider three cases:
\{|x|,|y|\geq 2\}
,
\{|x|<2\}
and
\{|y|<2\}
.

(i)

If
|x|,|y|\geq 2
then
\omega^{(\beta_{0}^{*})}(x)=\omega^{(\beta_{0}^{*})}(y)=0
.

(ii)

If
|x|<2
then

\displaystyle\frac{|\omega^{(\beta_{0}^{*})}(x)-\omega^{(\beta_{0}^{*})}(y)|}{|x-y|^{\beta^{*}-\beta_{0}^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=0}^{\beta_{0}^{*}}\binom{\beta_{0}^{*}}{j}(\beta^{*})_{j}\frac{\big||x|^{\beta^{*}-j}(\sgn x)^{j}\psi^{(\beta_{0}^{*}-j)}(x)-|y|^{\beta^{*}-j}(\sgn y)^{j}\psi^{(\beta_{0}^{*}-j)}(y)\big|}{|x-y|^{\beta^{*}-\beta_{0}^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=0}^{\beta_{0}^{*}}\binom{\beta_{0}^{*}}{j}(\beta^{*})_{j}\frac{|x|^{\beta^{*}-j}}{|x-y|^{\beta^{*}-\beta_{0}^{*}}}\big|\psi^{(\beta_{0}^{*}-j)}(x)-\psi^{(\beta_{0}^{*}-j)}(y)\big|

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\sum_{j=0}^{\beta_{0}^{*}}\binom{\beta_{0}^{*}}{j}(\beta^{*})_{j}\frac{\big||x|^{\beta^{*}-j}(\sgn x)^{j}-|y|^{\beta^{*}-j}(\sgn y)^{j}\big|}{|x-y|^{\beta^{*}-\beta_{0}^{*}}}\big|\psi^{(\beta_{0}^{*}-j)}(y)\big|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=0}^{\beta_{0}^{*}}L_{\beta_{0}^{*}-j,\beta^{*}-\beta_{0}^{*}}\binom{\beta_{0}^{*}}{j}(\beta^{*})_{j}2^{\beta^{*}-j}

\displaystyle\qquad+C_{\psi}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|y|<2\}}\sum_{j=0}^{\beta_{0}^{*}}\binom{\beta_{0}^{*}}{j}(\beta^{*})_{j}\frac{\big||x|^{\beta^{*}-j}(\sgn x)^{j}-|y|^{\beta^{*}-j}(\sgn y)^{j}\big|}{|x-y|^{\beta^{*}-\beta_{0}^{*}}}.

For
j\in\{0,1,\ldots,\beta_{0}^{*}\}
define
k_{j}(u):=|u|^{\beta^{*}-j}(\sgn u)^{j}
and

\bar{L}_{j}:=\bigl((\beta^{*}-j)2^{\beta^{*}-j-1}\bigr)\vee\|k_{\beta_{0}^{*}}\|_{C_{\beta^{*}-\beta_{0}^{*}}}<\infty.

Then when
|x|<2
and
|y|<2
,

\frac{\big||x|^{\beta^{*}-j}(\sgn x)^{j}-|y|^{\beta^{*}-j}(\sgn y)^{j}\big|}{|x-y|^{\beta^{*}-\beta_{0}^{*}}}\leq\bar{L}_{j}\frac{|x-y|\vee|x-y|^{\beta^{*}-\beta^{*}_{0}}}{|x-y|^{\beta^{*}-\beta_{0}^{*}}}\leq 4\bar{L}_{j}.

Therefore for
|x|<2
,

\frac{|\omega^{(\beta_{0}^{*})}(x)-\omega^{(\beta_{0}^{*})}(y)|}{|x-y|^{\beta^{*}-\beta_{0}^{*}}}\leq\sum_{j=0}^{\beta_{0}^{*}}2^{\beta^{*}-j}L_{\beta^{*}-j,\beta^{*}-\beta_{0}^{*}}\binom{\beta_{0}^{*}}{j}(\beta^{*})_{j}+4C_{\psi}\sum_{j=0}^{\beta_{0}^{*}}\bar{L}_{j}\binom{\beta_{0}^{*}}{j}(\beta^{*})_{j}<\infty.

(85)

(iii)

By symmetry (
85
) also holds when
|y|<2
.

We conclude that

\|\omega\|_{C_{\beta^{*}}}\leq\sum_{j=0}^{\beta_{0}^{*}}2^{\beta^{*}-j}L_{\beta^{*}-j,\beta^{*}-\beta_{0}^{*}}\binom{\beta_{0}^{*}}{j}(\beta^{*})_{j}+4C_{\psi}\sum_{j=0}^{\beta_{0}^{*}}\bar{L}_{j}\binom{\beta_{0}^{*}}{j}(\beta^{*})_{j}<\infty,

as required.
∎

Lemma 29
.

Recall the definition of
G_{M}
from (
76
). Then

(i)

For any
V,M>0
, the function
\mu\mapsto G_{M}(\mu,V)
is strictly increasing on
[0,\infty)
.

(ii)

For any
B_{\infty}>0
, there exists
M(B_{\infty}):=\frac{25}{4}B_{\infty}^{2}>0
such that for all
\mu\in[0,B_{\infty}]
and
M\geq M(B_{\infty})
, the function
V\mapsto G_{M}(\mu,V)
is strictly increasing on
[0,\infty)
.

(iii)

Fix any
M>0
. Then
G_{M}(\mu,V)\geq 0
, with equality if and only if
(\mu,V)=(0,0)
.

Proof.

(i)
We claim that non-central chi-squared distributions are stochastically ordered in the sense that for
\mu_{1}<\mu_{2}
and
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we have
\chi_{k}^{2}(\mu_{1})\leq_{\mathrm{st}}\chi_{k}^{2}(\mu_{2})
. To see this, let
Z_{1}\sim\mathrm{Poi}(\mu_{1}/2)
and
Z_{1}^{\prime}\sim\mathrm{Poi}\bigl((\mu_{2}-\mu_{1})/2\bigr)
be independent and let
Z_{2}:=Z_{1}+Z_{1}^{\prime}
. Now let
(E_{n})
be a sequence of independent
\mathrm{Exp}(1)
random variables that are independent of
(Z_{1},Z_{1}^{\prime})
, and set
S_{n}:=\sum_{i=1}^{n}E_{i}
for
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Then
Y_{1}:=S_{k+2Z_{1}}\sim\chi_{k}^{2}(\mu_{1})
and
Y_{2}:=S_{k+2Z_{2}}\sim\chi_{k}^{2}(\mu_{2})
, with
Y_{1}\leq Y_{2}
almost surely. This establishes the claim, and since
G_{M}(\mu,V)=\mathbb{E}\bigl\{V\chi_{1}^{2}(\mu^{2}/V)\wedge M\bigr\}
, the result follows.

(ii)
We have

\frac{\partial}{\partial V}G_{M}(\mu,V)=\frac{1}{2V}\mathbb{E}_{Z\sim N(\mu,V)}\bigg((Z^{2}\wedge M)\bigg\{\biggl(\frac{Z-\mu}{\sqrt{V}}\bigg)^{2}-1\bigg\}\bigg)=\frac{1}{2V}\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim N(0,1)}\Bigl(\bigl\{(\mu+\sqrt{V}\varepsilon)^{2}\wedge M\bigr\}(\varepsilon^{2}-1)\Bigr).

Fix
\mu\in[0,B_{\infty}]
and
M\geq M(B_{\infty})
, so that
\mu/\sqrt{M}\in[0,2/5]
.
Now

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim N(0,1)}\Bigl(\bigl\{(\mu+\sqrt{V}\varepsilon)^{2}\wedge M\bigr\}(\varepsilon^{2}-1)\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim N(0,1)}\Bigl(\min\bigl\{(\mu+\sqrt{V}\varepsilon)^{2}-M,0\bigr\}(\varepsilon^{2}-1)\Bigr)

\displaystyle=M\,\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim N(0,1)}\biggl(\min\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{\mu}{\sqrt{M}}+\sqrt{\frac{V}{M}}\varepsilon\biggr)^{2}-1,0\biggr\}(\varepsilon^{2}-1)\biggr),

so it suffices to show that for all
(x,y)\in(0,\infty)\times[0,2/5]
, we have

\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim N(0,1)}\Bigl(\min\bigl\{(y+x\varepsilon)^{2}-1,0\bigr\}(\varepsilon^{2}-1)\Bigr)>0.

(86)

By Stein’s lemma
(
56
, Lemma 1)
, and writing
\phi
and
\Phi
denote the standard normal density and distribution functions,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim N(0,1)}\Bigl(\min\bigl\{(y+x\varepsilon)^{2}

\displaystyle-1,0\bigr\}(\varepsilon^{2}-1)\Bigr)

\displaystyle=2x\,\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim N(0,1)}\Bigl(\mathbbm{1}_{\{|y+x\varepsilon|<1\}}\varepsilon(y+x\varepsilon)\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\int_{\frac{-1-y}{x}}^{\frac{1-y}{x}}\varepsilon(y+x\varepsilon)\phi(\varepsilon)\,d\varepsilon

\displaystyle=-\phi\bigg(\frac{1-y}{x}\bigg)-\phi\bigg(\frac{-1-y}{x}\bigg)+x\bigg\{\Phi\bigg(\frac{1-y}{x}\bigg)-\Phi\bigg(\frac{-1-y}{x}\bigg)\bigg\}

\displaystyle=x\bigg\{-\frac{1}{x}\phi\bigg(\frac{1-y}{x}\bigg)-\frac{1}{x}\phi\bigg(\frac{1+y}{x}\bigg)+\Phi\bigg(\frac{1-y}{x}\bigg)+\Phi\bigg(\frac{1+y}{x}\bigg)-1\bigg\}

\displaystyle=xF_{y}\bigg(\frac{1}{x}\bigg),

where

F_{y}(x):=\Phi\big((1+y)x\big)+\Phi\big((1-y)x\big)-x\phi\big((1+y)x\big)-x\phi\big((1-y)x\big)-1.

We have
F_{y}(0)=2\Phi(0)-1=0
, and
F_{y}
is continuous for each
y\in[0,2/5]
. Moreover,

\displaystyle F_{y}^{\prime}(x)

\displaystyle=(1+y)\phi\big((1+y)x\big)+(1-y)\phi\big((1-y)x\big)-\phi\big((1+y)x\big)-x(1+y)\phi^{\prime}\big((1+y)x\big)

\displaystyle\qquad-\phi\bigl((1-y)x\bigr)-x(1-y)\phi^{\prime}\bigl((1-y)x\bigr)

\displaystyle=\bigl(y+(1+y)^{2}x^{2}\bigr)\phi\big((1+y)x\big)+\bigl(-y+(1-y)^{2}x^{2}\bigr)\phi\big((1-y)x\big)

\displaystyle=:\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\exp\biggl(-\frac{(1+y)^{2}x^{2}}{2}\biggr)G_{y}(x^{2}),

where

G_{y}(\nu):=\bigl(y+(1+y)^{2}\nu\bigr)+\bigl(-y+(1-y)^{2}\nu\bigr)e^{2y\nu}.

Now
G_{y}(0)=0
, and
G_{y}
is continuous for each
y\in[0,2/5]
. Moreover,

\displaystyle G_{y}^{\prime}(\nu)

\displaystyle=(1+y)^{2}+\big((1-y)^{2}-2y^{2}+2y(1-y)^{2}\nu\big)e^{2y\nu}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{(1+y)^{2}}_{\geq 1}+\underbrace{\big(1-2y-y^{2}\big)}_{\geq 1/25\text{ for }y\in[0,2/5]}\,\underbrace{e^{2y\nu}}_{\geq 1}+\underbrace{2y(1-y)^{2}}_{\geq 0}\,\underbrace{\nu e^{2y\nu}}_{\geq 0}\geq\frac{26}{25}>0.

Therefore
G_{y}(\nu)>0
for all
(\nu,y)\in(0,\infty)\times[0,2/5]
, so
F_{y}(x)>0
for all
(x,y)\in(0,\infty)\times[0,2/5]
, and so (
86
) holds, from which
(ii)
follows.

(iii)
The fact that
G_{M}(\mu,V)\geq 0
follows immediately from the definition. If
V>0
then
G_{M}(\mu,V)=\mathbb{E}_{Z\sim N(\mu,V)}(Z^{2}\wedge M)>0
, as
z^{2}\wedge M>0
for
z\neq 0
. Finally
G_{M}(\mu,0)=\mu^{2}\wedge M=0
if and only if
\mu=0
.
∎

Lemma 30
.

Fix
x_{0}\in\mathcal{X}
and assume that the conditional density
p(\cdot\,|\,x_{0})
of
\varepsilon\,|\,X=x_{0}
is absolutely continuous with respect to Lebesgue measure with
i_{P}(x_{0})<\infty
. Then

V_{P,x_{0}}^{(\infty)}(\cdot)
is minimised over absolutely continuous functions
\varrho(\cdot\,|\,x_{0})
satisfying
\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl\{\varrho^{2}(\varepsilon\,|\,X)\,\big|\,X=x_{0}\bigr\}<\infty
by
\varrho(\cdot\,|\,x_{0})=\rho(\cdot\,|\,x_{0})
, where
\rho(\cdot\,|\,x_{0}):=p^{\prime}(\cdot\,|\,x_{0})/p(\cdot\,|\,x_{0})
is the conditional score function.

Proof.

The fact that
p(\cdot\,|\,x_{0})
is absolutely continuous means that
\lim_{\varepsilon\to\pm\infty}p(\varepsilon\,|\,x_{0})=0
. Fix
t_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
, and define
g(\varepsilon,t):=\mathbbm{1}_{\{\varepsilon<t\leq t_{0}\}}-\mathbbm{1}_{\{t_{0}<t\leq\varepsilon\}}
for
\varepsilon,t\in\mathbb{R}
. Then
\int_{\mathbb{R}}g(\varepsilon,t)\varrho^{\prime}(t\,|\,x_{0})\,dt=\varrho(t_{0}\,|\,x_{0})-\varrho(\varepsilon\,|\,x_{0})
for all
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}
. Moreover
\int_{\mathbb{R}}p^{\prime}(\varepsilon\,|\,x_{0})g(\varepsilon,t)\,d\varepsilon=p(t\,|\,x_{0})
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
. By Cauchy–Schwarz,

\bigl[\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl\{\varrho(\varepsilon\,|\,X)\rho(\varepsilon\,|\,X)\,|\,X=x_{0}\bigr\}\bigr]^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl\{\varrho^{2}(\varepsilon\,|\,X)\,\big|\,X=x_{0}\bigr\}\cdot i_{P}(x_{0})<\infty.

Hence, applying Fubini’s theorem,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl\{\varrho(\varepsilon

\displaystyle\,|\,X)\rho(\varepsilon\,|\,X)\,|\,X=x_{0}\bigr\}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varrho(\varepsilon\,|\,x_{0})p^{\prime}(\varepsilon\,|\,x_{0})\,d\varepsilon

\displaystyle=\varrho(t_{0}\,|\,x_{0})\int_{\mathbb{R}}p^{\prime}(\varepsilon\,|\,x_{0})\,d\varepsilon-\int_{\mathbb{R}}p^{\prime}(\varepsilon\,|\,x_{0})\int_{\mathbb{R}}g(\varepsilon,t)\varrho^{\prime}(t\,|\,x_{0})\,dt\,d\varepsilon

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varrho^{\prime}(t\,|\,x_{0})\int_{\mathbb{R}}p^{\prime}(\varepsilon\,|\,x_{0})g(\varepsilon,t)\,d\varepsilon\,dt=-\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl\{\varrho^{\prime}(\varepsilon\,|\,X)\,|\,X)\,|\,X=x_{0}\bigr\}.

(87)

Therefore

\displaystyle V_{P,x_{0}}^{(\infty)}(\varrho)=\frac{\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl\{\varrho^{2}(\varepsilon\,|\,X)\,|\,X=x_{0}\bigr\}}{\bigl\{\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl(\varrho^{\prime}(\varepsilon\,|\,X)\,|\,X=x_{0}\bigr)\bigr\}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl\{\varrho^{2}(\varepsilon\,|\,X)\,|\,X=x_{0}\bigr\}}{\bigl\{\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl(\varrho(\varepsilon\,|\,X)\rho(\varepsilon\,|\,X)\,|\,X=x_{0}\bigr)\bigr\}^{2}}\geq\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl\{\rho^{2}(\varepsilon\,|\,X)\,|\,X=x_{0}\bigr\}},

where the inequality follows by Cauchy–Schwarz, and equality holds if and only if
\varrho(\cdot\,|\,x_{0})\propto\rho(\cdot\,|\,x_{0})
.
∎

Lemma 31
.

Adopt the setup of Lemma
30
, and assume that
\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow\pm\infty}\varepsilon p(\varepsilon\,|\,x_{0})=0
. Then

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{\lambda\in\Lambda_{n}}V_{P,x_{0}}^{(\lambda)}(\rho)\leq\sigma_{P}^{2}(x_{0}),

with equality if and only if
\rho(\cdot\,|\,x_{0})
is linear.

Proof.

Recall the definition

\displaystyle V_{P,x_{0}}^{(\lambda)}(\rho)

\displaystyle=V_{P,x_{0}}^{(\infty)}(\rho)+\bigl(\sigma_{P}^{2}(x_{0})-V_{P,x_{0}}^{(\infty)}(\rho)\bigr)\frac{R_{2}(\kappa_{\lambda})}{R_{2}(K)}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{P}^{2}(x_{0})-\biggl(1-\frac{R_{2}(\kappa_{\lambda})}{R_{2}(K)}\biggr)\bigl(\sigma_{P}^{2}(x_{0})-V_{P,x_{0}}^{(\infty)}(\rho)\bigr).

Define
\mathrm{id}:\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
by
\mathrm{id}(\varepsilon\,|\,x):=\varepsilon
. Then, by Lemma
30
,
V_{P,x_{0}}^{(\infty)}(\rho)\leq V_{P,x_{0}}^{(\infty)}(\mathrm{id})=\sigma_{P}^{2}(x_{0})
, and so

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{\lambda\in\Lambda_{n}}V_{P,x_{0}}^{(\lambda)}(\rho)=\sigma_{P}^{2}(x_{0})-\biggl(1-\frac{\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{\lambda\in\Lambda_{n}}R_{2}(\kappa_{\lambda})}{R_{2}(K)}\biggr)\bigl(\sigma_{P}^{2}(x_{0})-V_{P,x_{0}}^{(\infty)}(\rho)\bigr)\leq\sigma_{P}^{2}(x_{0}),

with equality if and only if
\rho(\cdot\,|\,x_{0})\propto\mathrm{id}(\cdot\,|\,x_{0})
.
∎

Appendix D
Proof of Theorem
4

D.1
Proof of Theorem
4
(i)

Adopt the notation of the proof of Theorem
3
. There, the following two facts are established. First, there exists
\mu_{\max}:=C_{B}(K,d,\beta_{0}^{*},L)>0

(defined in Lemma
15
(ii)
) such that

\sup_{f\in\mathcal{H}(\beta,L)}\sup_{\epsilon>0}\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}_{n}}\bar{B}_{f,\epsilon,x_{0},K,h}\,\leq\mu_{\max}.

Second, for any
\eta\in(0,\frac{1}{4}C_{G}]
there exists
N\in\mathbb{N}
, not depending on
(P,x_{0},h,\lambda)
, such that for any
n\geq N
,
M\geq\frac{25}{4}C_{B}(K,d,\beta_{0}^{*},L)^{2}
and error distribution
P_{\varepsilon|x_{0}}
,

\displaystyle\quad\;\sup_{P_{X}\in\mathcal{P}_{X}}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{H}(\beta,L)}\sup_{(h,\lambda)\in\mathcal{H}_{n}^{\prime}\times\Lambda_{n}}\frac{\mathcal{R}_{n,h,P,x_{0},\epsilon,M}(\hat{f}_{n}^{\mathrm{Outrig}})}{\mathcal{R}_{n,h,P,x_{0},\epsilon,M}(\hat{f}_{n}^{\mathrm{LP}})}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{P_{X}\in\mathcal{P}_{X}}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{H}(\beta,L)}\sup_{(h,\lambda)\in\mathcal{H}_{n}^{\prime}\times\Lambda_{n}}\!\!\frac{G_{M}\Big(\bar{B}_{f,\epsilon,x_{0},K,h}\,\alpha_{1}\big(nh^{2\beta^{*}\hskip-0.81949pt+d}\big),\,\frac{R_{2}(K)V_{P,x_{0}}^{(\lambda)}(\rho)}{p_{X}(x_{0})}\alpha_{2}\big(nh^{2\beta^{*}\hskip-0.81949pt+d}\big)\Big)+2\eta}{G_{M}\Big(\bar{B}_{f,\epsilon,x_{0},K,h}\,\alpha_{1}\big(nh^{2\beta^{*}\hskip-0.81949pt+d}\big),\,\frac{R_{2}(K)\sigma_{P}^{2}(x_{0})}{p_{X}(x_{0})}\alpha_{2}\big(nh^{2\beta^{*}\hskip-0.81949pt+d}\big)\Big)-2\eta}.

Write
\gamma:=\inf_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\inf_{\lambda\in\Lambda_{n}}\bigl(1-\frac{R_{2}(\kappa_{\lambda})}{R_{2}(K)}\bigr)>0
and
\theta:=\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}_{n}^{\prime}}nh^{2\beta^{*}\hskip-0.81949pt+d}<\infty
.
We now apply Lemma
32
with
\xi:=\frac{R_{2}(K)}{p_{X}(x_{0})}\alpha_{2}\big(nh^{2\beta^{*}\hskip-0.81949pt+d}\big)
so that
\xi\geq\frac{R_{2}(K)}{C_{X}}\alpha_{2}(\theta)=:\xi_{\min}>0
and
\xi\leq\frac{R_{2}(K)}{c_{X}}=:\xi_{\max}
, as well as
\mu=\bar{B}_{f,\epsilon,x_{0},K,h}\alpha_{1}\bigl(nh^{2\beta^{*}\hskip-0.81949pt+d}\bigr)\leq\mu_{\max}
so that
|\mu|\leq\mu_{\max}
, and
V_{1}:=V_{P,x_{0}}^{(\lambda)}(\rho)=1/i_{P}(x_{0})+\frac{R_{2}(\kappa_{\lambda})}{R_{2}(K)}\bigl(\sigma_{P}^{2}(x_{0})-1/i_{P}(x_{0})\bigr)
,
V_{2}:=\sigma_{P}^{2}(x_{0})
. Then

\displaystyle\sup_{P_{X}\in\mathcal{P}_{X}}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{H}(\beta,L)}

\displaystyle\sup_{(h,\lambda)\in\mathcal{H}_{n}^{\prime}\times\Lambda_{n}}\frac{\mathcal{R}_{n,h,P,x_{0},\epsilon,M}(\hat{f}_{n}^{\mathrm{Outrig}})}{\mathcal{R}_{n,h,P,x_{0},\epsilon,M}(\hat{f}_{n}^{\mathrm{LP}})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1-\Delta}\sup_{\lambda\in\Lambda_{n}}\biggl(1+\frac{\xi_{\min}\gamma\bigl(\sigma_{P}^{2}(x_{0})-\frac{1}{i_{P}(x_{0})}\bigr)}{\mu_{\max}^{2}+\xi_{\max}V_{P,x_{0}}^{(\lambda)}(\rho)+2\eta}-\frac{2\big(1+\tfrac{1}{1-\Delta}\big)\eta}{\xi_{\min}V_{P,x_{0}}^{(\lambda)}(\rho)+2\eta}\biggr)^{-1}

(88)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1-\Delta}\biggl(1+\frac{\xi_{\min}\gamma\bigl(\sigma_{P}^{2}(x_{0})-\frac{1}{i_{P}(x_{0})}\bigr)}{\mu_{\max}^{2}+\xi_{\max}\sigma_{P}^{2}(x_{0})+2\eta}-\frac{2\big(1+\tfrac{1}{1-\Delta}\big)\eta}{\xi_{\min}/i_{P}(x_{0})+2\eta}\biggr)^{-1}.

Since
\Delta\in(0,1)
and
\eta\in(0,C_{G}/4]
were arbitrary,

\displaystyle{r}_{\mathcal{H}^{\prime}}(P_{\varepsilon|x_{0}})\leq\biggl(1+\frac{\xi_{\min}\gamma\bigl(\sigma_{P}^{2}(x_{0})-\frac{1}{i_{P}(x_{0})}\bigr)}{\mu_{\max}^{2}+\xi_{\max}\sigma_{P}^{2}(x_{0})}\biggr)^{-1}\leq 1,

(89)

with equality if and only if
\sigma_{P}^{2}(x_{0})=\frac{1}{i_{P}(x_{0})}
, which occurs only if
P_{\varepsilon|x_{0}}
is Gaussian by Lemma
31
. ∎

D.2
Proof of Theorem
4
(ii)

Adopt the notation of the proof of Theorem
3
, as well as the quantities
\xi_{\min},\xi_{\max}
and
\mu_{\max}
as in the proof of Theorem
4
(i)
.
Given
\tau\in(3,5]
, define a distribution
P_{\varepsilon|x_{0}}(\tau)
for
\tau\in(3,5]
with density function

p_{\varepsilon|x_{0}}(u\,|\,x_{0};\tau):=\frac{C_{\tau}}{|u|^{\tau}+1},\qquad\text{where}\ C_{\tau}:=\frac{\tau\sin(\pi/\tau)}{2\pi}.

Then

\sigma_{P(\tau)}^{2}(x_{0})=\mathbb{E}_{P(\tau)}(\varepsilon^{2}\,|\,X=x_{0})=2C_{\tau}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{u^{2}}{u^{\tau}+1}\,du=\frac{\sin(\pi/\tau)}{\sin(3\pi/\tau)}.

(90)

Further, the conditional score function is

\rho(\varepsilon\,|\,x_{0};\tau)=\frac{-p^{\prime}(\varepsilon\,|\,x_{0};\tau)}{p(\varepsilon\,|\,x_{0};\tau)}=\frac{\tau|\varepsilon|^{\tau-1}\sgn\varepsilon}{|\varepsilon|^{\tau}+1},

and so

\frac{1}{i_{P(\tau)}(x_{0})}=\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}_{P(\tau)}\{\rho^{2}(\varepsilon\,|\,x_{0};\tau)\}}=\bigg(2C_{\tau}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\tau^{2}u^{2(\tau-1)}}{(u^{\tau}+1)^{3}}\,du\bigg)^{-1}=\frac{2}{\tau-1}\in[1/2,2).

(91)

Now define
\gamma(\underline{\lambda}):=1-\frac{R_{2}(\kappa_{\underline{\lambda}})}{R_{2}(K)}
.
Arguing similarly to (
88
), we have for each
\underline{\lambda}>\lambda_{0}(K)
,
\Delta\in(0,1)
and
\eta\in(0,C_{G}/4]
that

\displaystyle r_{\mathcal{H}^{\prime}}\bigl(P_{\varepsilon|x_{0}}(\tau),\underline{\lambda}\bigr)\leq\frac{1}{1-\Delta}\biggl(1+\frac{\xi_{\min}\gamma(\underline{\lambda})\bigl(\sigma_{P}^{2}(x_{0})-\frac{1}{i_{P}(x_{0})}\bigr)}{\mu_{\max}^{2}+\xi_{\max}V_{P,x_{0}}^{(\underline{\lambda})}(\rho)+2\eta}-\frac{2\big(1+\tfrac{1}{1-\Delta}\big)\eta}{\xi_{\min}V_{P,x_{0}}^{(\underline{\lambda})}(\rho)+2\eta}\biggr)^{-1}.

Since
\Delta\in(0,1)
and
\eta\in(0,C_{G}/4]
were arbitrary, we deduce from (
90
) and (
91
) that

\displaystyle r_{\mathcal{H}^{\prime}}\bigl(P_{\varepsilon|x_{0}}(\tau),\underline{\lambda}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\biggl(1+\frac{\xi_{\min}\gamma(\underline{\lambda})\bigl(\sigma_{P(\tau)}^{2}(x_{0})-\frac{1}{i_{P(\tau)}(x_{0})}\bigr)}{\mu_{\max}^{2}+\xi_{\max}\bigl\{\frac{1}{i_{P(\tau)}(x_{0})}+\bigl(1-\gamma(\underline{\lambda})\bigr)\bigl(\sigma_{P(\tau)}^{2}(x_{0})-\frac{1}{i_{P(\tau)}(x_{0})}\bigr)\bigr\}}\biggr)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\biggl(1+\frac{\xi_{\min}\gamma(\underline{\lambda})}{\mu_{\max}^{2}+\xi_{\max}\bigl\{\frac{2}{\tau-1}+\bigl(1-\gamma(\underline{\lambda})\bigr)\bigl(\frac{\sin(\pi/\tau)}{\sin(3\pi/\tau)}-\frac{2}{\tau-1}\bigr)\bigr\}}\biggl\{\frac{\sin(\pi/\tau)}{\sin(3\pi/\tau)}-\frac{2}{\tau-1}\biggr\}\biggr)^{-1}.

Since
\gamma:[\lambda_{0}(K),\infty)\to(0,1]
is a strictly increasing function with
\gamma\bigl(\lambda_{0}(K)\bigr)=0
and
\lim_{\underline{\lambda}\to\infty}\gamma(\underline{\lambda})=1
, and
\frac{\sin(\pi/\tau)}{\sin(3\pi/\tau)}-\frac{2}{\tau-1}\to\infty
as
\tau\searrow 3
, we conclude that

\lim_{\tau\searrow 3,\,\underline{\lambda}\to\infty}r_{\mathcal{H}^{\prime}}\bigl(P_{\varepsilon|x_{0}}(\tau),\underline{\lambda}\bigr)=0,

so the result follows.
∎

D.3
Additional lemma for Theorem
4

Lemma 32
.

Fix
\Delta\in(0,1)
and
\eta\in(0,C_{G}/4]
, as well as
0<\xi_{\min}\leq\xi_{\max}
,
\mu_{\max}\in\mathbb{R}
and
0<V_{\min}\leq V_{\max}
. Suppose that
\xi\in[\xi_{\min},\xi_{\max}]
,
\mu\in[-\mu_{\max},\mu_{\max}]
and
V_{1},V_{2}\in[V_{\min},V_{\max}]
with
V_{1}\leq V_{2}
and
M\geq 3(\mu_{\max}^{2}+\xi_{\max}V_{\max})/\Delta
. Then

\frac{G_{M}(\mu,\xi V_{1})+2\eta}{G_{M}(\mu,\xi V_{2})-2\eta}\leq\frac{1}{1-\Delta}\bigg(1+\frac{\xi_{\min}(V_{2}-V_{1})}{\mu_{\max}^{2}+\xi_{\max}V_{1}+2\eta}-\frac{2\big(1+\tfrac{1}{1-\Delta}\big)\eta}{\xi_{\min}V_{1}+2\eta}\bigg)^{-1}.

(92)

Proof.

For any
\mu\in\mathbb{R}
and
V\geq 0
, we have

G_{M}(\mu,V)=\mu^{2}+V-\mathbb{E}_{Z\sim N(\mu,V)}\bigl\{(Z^{2}-M)\vee 0\bigr\},

so
G_{M}(\mu,V)\leq\mu^{2}+V
. Further,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{Z\sim N(\mu,V)}\bigl\{(Z^{2}-M)\vee 0\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{Z\sim N(\mu,V)}\bigl(Z^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|Z|>\sqrt{M}\}}\bigr)-M\mathbb{P}(|Z|>\sqrt{M})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{M}\mathbb{E}_{Z\sim N(\mu,V)}(Z^{4})=\frac{\mu^{4}+6\mu^{2}V+3V^{2}}{M},

so

\mu^{2}+V-\frac{\mu^{4}+6\mu^{2}V+3V^{2}}{M}\leq G_{M}(\mu,V)\leq\mu^{2}+V.

On the other hand,

\frac{\mu^{4}+6\mu^{2}\xi V_{2}+3\xi^{2}V_{2}^{2}}{M}\leq\frac{3(\mu^{2}+\xi V_{2})^{2}}{M}\leq\Delta(\mu^{2}+\xi V_{2}),

so

\displaystyle G_{M}(\mu,\xi V_{2})

\displaystyle\geq\mu^{2}+\xi V_{2}-\frac{\mu^{4}+6\mu^{2}\xi V_{2}+3\xi^{2}V_{2}^{2}}{M}\geq(1-\Delta)(\mu^{2}+\xi V_{2}).

Therefore

\displaystyle\frac{G_{M}(\mu,\xi V_{1})+2\eta}{G_{M}(\mu,\xi V_{2})-2\eta}\leq\frac{1}{1\!-\!\Delta}\biggl(\frac{\mu^{2}+\xi V_{2}-\tfrac{2\eta}{1-\Delta}}{\mu^{2}+\xi V_{1}+2\eta}\biggr)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1\!-\!\Delta}\biggl(1+\frac{\xi(V_{2}-V_{1})}{\mu^{2}+\xi V_{1}+2\eta}-\frac{2\big(1+\tfrac{1}{1-\Delta}\big)\eta}{\mu^{2}+\xi V_{1}+2\eta}\biggr)^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1\!-\!\Delta}\biggl(1+\frac{\xi_{\min}(V_{2}-V_{1})}{\mu_{\max}^{2}+\xi_{\max}V_{1}+2\eta}-\frac{2\big(1+\tfrac{1}{1-\Delta}\big)\eta}{\xi_{\min}V_{1}+2\eta}\biggr)^{-1},

as required.
∎

Appendix E
Proofs of minimaxity results of Section
3.2

Proof of Theorem
5
.

We take
K
to be a bounded kernel of order
p+1
supported on
\mathcal{B}_{0}(1)
, and define

\displaystyle\bar{\mu}_{\beta}(K)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathcal{B}_{0}(1)}|K(\nu)|\,\|\nu\|_{1}^{\beta_{0}}\|\nu\|^{\beta-\beta_{0}}\,d\nu,\qquad a:=\frac{d\,\Gamma^{2}(\beta+1)R_{2}(K)}{2\beta\Gamma^{2}(\beta-\beta_{0}+1)L^{2}\bar{\mu}_{\beta}^{2}(K)i_{P}(x_{0})p_{X}(x_{0})},

\displaystyle h\equiv h_{n}

\displaystyle:=a^{1/(2\beta+d)}n^{-1/(2\beta+d)}.

Consider the decomposition of Theorem
1
.
In the case
\beta\in(0,1]
, the bias term (
39
) satisfies

\displaystyle|B(f,x_{0},K,h)|\,h^{\beta}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathcal{B}_{0}(1)}|K(\nu)|\,|f(x_{0}+h\nu)-f(x_{0})|\,d\nu\leq L\int_{\mathcal{B}_{0}(1)}|K(\nu)|\,\|h\nu\|^{\beta}\,d\nu\leq Lh^{\beta}\bar{\mu}_{\beta}(K).

On the other hand, for
\beta>1
we have from (
65
) that

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}:\\
\|\alpha\|_{1}=\beta_{0}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\alpha!}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{0}(1)}|K(\nu)|\,|\nu^{\alpha}|\,\|\nu\|^{\beta-\beta_{0}}\,d\nu=\frac{1}{\beta_{0}!}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{0}(1)}|K(\nu)|\,\|\nu\|_{1}^{\beta_{0}}\|\nu\|^{\beta-\beta_{0}}\,d\nu=\frac{\bar{\mu}_{\beta}(K)}{\beta_{0}!}.

Thus, the bias term (
40
) satisfies

\displaystyle|B(f,x_{0},K,h)|\,h^{\beta}

\displaystyle\leq h^{\beta_{0}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}:\\
\|\alpha\|_{1}=\beta_{0}\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{0}}{\alpha!}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{0}(1)}|K(\nu)|\,|\nu^{\alpha}|\,\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{\beta_{0}-1}\bigl|\partial^{\alpha}f(x_{0}+th\nu)-\partial^{\alpha}f(x_{0})\bigr|\,dt\,d\nu

\displaystyle\leq Lh^{\beta}\int_{0}^{1}t^{\beta-\beta_{0}}(1-t)^{\beta_{0}-1}\,dt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}:\\
\|\alpha\|_{1}=\beta_{0}\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{0}}{\alpha!}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{0}(1)}|K(\nu)|\,|\nu^{\alpha}|\,\|\nu\|^{\beta-\beta_{0}}\,d\nu

\displaystyle=Lh^{\beta}\frac{\Gamma(\beta-\beta_{0}+1)}{\Gamma(\beta+1)}\bar{\mu}_{\beta}(K).

Using the fact that
V_{P,x_{0}}^{(\infty)}(\rho)=1/i_{P}(x_{0})
, and arguing as in the proof of Theorem
3
, we therefore have

\displaystyle\limsup_{M\to\infty}

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathrm{MSE}_{n,M}(\hat{f}^{\mathrm{Outrig}}_{n})\leq L^{2}a^{2\beta/(2\beta+d)}\frac{\Gamma^{2}(\beta-\beta_{0}+1)}{\Gamma^{2}(\beta+1)}\bar{\mu}_{\beta}^{2}(K)+\frac{R_{2}(K)}{i_{P}(x_{0})p_{X}(x_{0})\,a^{d/(2\beta+d)}}

\displaystyle=(2\beta+d)\biggl(\frac{1}{(2\beta)^{2\beta}d^{d}}\frac{\Gamma^{2d}(\beta-\beta_{0}+1)}{\Gamma^{2d}(\beta+1)}\bar{\mu}_{\beta}^{2d}(K)R_{2}^{2\beta}(K)\biggr)^{1/(2\beta+d)}\biggl(\frac{L^{d/\beta}}{i_{P}(x_{0})p_{X}(x_{0})}\biggr)^{2\beta/(2\beta+d)}.

This establishes (
20
), and it remains to bound
C_{\beta,1}
. Now

\bar{\mu}_{\beta}(K)=\int_{\mathcal{B}_{0}(1)}|K(\nu)|\,\|\nu\|_{1}^{\beta_{0}}\|\nu\|^{\beta-\beta_{0}}\,d\nu\leq d^{\beta_{0}(q-1)/q}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{0}(1)}|K(\nu)|\|\nu\|^{\beta}\,d\nu=:d^{\beta_{0}(q-1)/q}\tilde{\mu}_{\beta}(K).

Henceforth we take our kernel
K
to be

K(\nu):=\frac{\beta+d}{\beta}\frac{\Gamma(1+d/q)}{2^{d}\Gamma^{d}(1+1/q)}\max\bigl(0,\,1-\|\nu\|^{\beta}\bigr),

so that

R_{2}(K)=\frac{2(\beta+d)}{2\beta+d}\cdot\frac{\Gamma(1+d/q)}{2^{d}\Gamma^{d}(1+1/q)},\qquad\tilde{\mu}_{\beta}(K)=\frac{d}{2\beta+d}.

Hence

\tilde{\mu}_{\beta}^{d}(K)R_{2}^{\beta}(K)=\frac{d^{d}(\beta+d)^{\beta}}{2^{\beta(d-1)}(2\beta+d)^{\beta+d}}\cdot\frac{\Gamma^{\beta}(1+d/q)}{\Gamma^{\beta d}(1+d/q)}.

Moreover, for
\beta\in(0,2]
, we have
\frac{\Gamma(\beta+1)}{\Gamma(\beta-\beta_{0}+1)}=1\vee\beta
,
and so (
20
) holds with

\displaystyle C_{\beta,d}

\displaystyle=\biggl\{\frac{d^{(2\beta_{0}(q-1)/q+1)d}(\beta+d)^{2\beta}}{(2^{d}\beta)^{2\beta}(2\beta+d)^{d}(1\vee\beta)^{2d}}\cdot\frac{\Gamma^{2\beta}(1+d/q)}{\Gamma^{2\beta d}(1+1/q)}\biggr\}^{1/(2\beta+d)},

(93)

and in the special case
d=1
, this reduces to (
21
).
∎

For our corresponding lower bound we will make use of the following lemma. Our explicit constants involve the hypergeometric function
{}_{2}F_{1}
, where, for
a\in\mathbb{R}
,
c>b>0
and
z\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

{}_{2}F_{1}(a,b,c;z):=\frac{1}{\mathrm{B}(b,c-b)}\int_{0}^{1}x^{b-1}(1-x)^{c-b-1}(1-zx)^{-a}\,dx,

where
\mathrm{B}(\cdot,\cdot)
denotes the beta function. For more details on hypergeometric functions, see e.g.,
1
. We also introduce the following notation. Given a function
K:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
we define the
\beta
-Hölder semi-norm

\|K\|_{C_{\beta}}:=\sup_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}\,:\,\|\alpha\|_{1}=\beta_{0}}\sup_{x\neq y}\frac{|\partial^{\alpha}K(x)-\partial^{\alpha}K(y)|}{\|x-y\|^{\beta-\beta_{0}}},

where
\beta_{0}:=\lceil\beta\rceil-1
.

Lemma 33
.

Let
x_{0}\in\mathcal{X}
and
\beta\in(0,\infty)
.
Fix an arbitrary function
K:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
with
\|K\|_{C_{\beta}}<\infty
, as well as
\sup_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}\,:\,\|\alpha\|_{1}\leq\beta_{0}}\|\partial^{\alpha}K\|_{\infty}<\infty
. Then for any sequence
(\hat{f}_{n})
of estimators,

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathrm{MSE}_{n}(\hat{f}_{n})\geq c_{\beta,d}(K)\biggl(\frac{L^{d/\beta}}{p_{X}(x_{0})i_{P}(x_{0})}\biggr)^{2\beta/(2\beta+d)},

(94)

where

c_{\beta,d}(K):=a_{\beta,d}\cdot\biggl(\frac{(2\beta)^{2\beta}d^{d}}{(2\beta+d)^{2\beta+d}}\biggr)^{1/(2\beta+d)}\frac{K^{2}(0)}{\|K\|_{C_{\beta}}^{2d/(2\beta+d)}R_{2}(K)^{2\beta/(2\beta+d)}},

and
a_{\beta,d}>0
satisfies

\frac{1}{1.69}\leq\inf_{\beta\in(0,\infty)}\inf_{d\in\mathbb{N}}a_{\beta,d}\leq\lim_{\beta\searrow 0}\inf_{d\in\mathbb{N}}a_{\beta,d}=1.

(95)

Proof of Lemma
33
.

For
t\in[-1,1]
, define
f_{t}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
by

f_{t}(x):=\frac{tLh^{\beta}}{\|K\|_{C_{\beta}}}K\Bigl(\frac{x-x_{0}}{h}\Bigr),

(96)

with

h:=\biggl(\frac{\lambda\|K\|_{C_{\beta}}^{2}}{L^{2}R_{2}(K)p_{X}(x_{0})i_{P}(x_{0})n}\biggr)^{1/(2\beta+d)}

(97)

for some
\lambda>0
. Then for all
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
with
\|\alpha\|_{1}\leq\beta_{0}
,

\|\partial^{\alpha}f_{t}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{L\|\partial^{\alpha}K\|_{\infty}h^{\beta-\|\alpha\|_{1}}}{\|K\|_{C_{\beta}}}\to 0

as
n\to\infty
.
Moreover,

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}:\\
\|\alpha\|_{1}=\beta_{0}\end{subarray}}\sup_{x\neq y}\frac{|\partial^{\alpha}f_{t}(x)-\partial^{\alpha}f_{t}(y)|}{\|x-y\|^{\beta-\beta_{0}}}\leq\frac{Lh^{\beta-\beta_{0}}}{\|K\|_{C_{\beta}}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}:\\
\|\alpha\|_{1}=\beta_{0}\end{subarray}}\sup_{x\neq y}\frac{|\partial^{\alpha}K(\frac{x-y}{h})-\partial^{\alpha}K(\frac{y-x_{0}}{h})|}{\|x-y\|^{\beta-\beta_{0}}}=L.

Hence there exists
N\in\mathbb{N}
such that
f_{t}\in\mathcal{H}(\beta,L)
for all
t\in[-1,1]
and
n\geq N
. Let
P_{t}
denote the joint distribution of independent and identically distributed pairs
(X_{1},Y_{1}),\ldots,(X_{n},Y_{n})
satisfying
X_{1}\sim P_{X}
and

Y_{1}=f_{t}(X_{1})+\varepsilon_{1},

(98)

where
\varepsilon_{1}\,|\,X_{1}\sim P_{\varepsilon|X}
. Suppose further that
P_{t}:=(P_{X},P_{\varepsilon|X},f_{t})\in\mathcal{P}
. The Fisher information
I(t)
of
P_{t}
is given by

\displaystyle I(t)

\displaystyle=n\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\biggl(\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\log\bigl\{p_{\varepsilon|X}\bigl(y-f_{t}(x)\,|\,x\bigr)p_{X}(x)\bigr\}\biggr)^{2}p_{\varepsilon|X}\bigl(y-f_{t}(x)\,|\,x\bigr)p_{X}(x)\,dy\,dx

\displaystyle=\frac{nL^{2}h^{2\beta}}{\|K\|_{C_{\beta}}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\bigl\{p_{\varepsilon|X}^{\prime}\bigl(y-f_{t}(x)\,|\,x\bigr)\bigr\}^{2}}{p_{\varepsilon|X}\bigl(y-f_{t}(x)\,|\,x\bigr)}K^{2}\Bigl(\frac{x-x_{0}}{h}\Bigr)p_{X}(x)\,dy\,dx

\displaystyle=\frac{nL^{2}h^{2\beta}}{\|K\|_{C_{\beta}}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbb{E}_{P_{\varepsilon|X}}\bigl\{\rho^{2}(\varepsilon\,|\,X)\,\big|\,X=x\bigr\}K^{2}\Bigl(\frac{x-x_{0}}{h}\Bigr)p_{X}(x)\,dx

\displaystyle=\frac{nL^{2}h^{2\beta+d}}{\|K\|_{C_{\beta}}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbb{E}_{P_{\varepsilon|X}}\bigl\{\rho^{2}(\varepsilon\,|\,X)\,\big|\,X=x_{0}+h\nu\bigr\}K^{2}(\nu)p_{X}(x_{0}+h\nu)\,d\nu

\displaystyle\to\lambda,

as
n\to\infty
by the dominated convergence theorem, where we recall that
i_{P}(x_{0}):=\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl(\rho^{2}(\varepsilon\,|\,X)\,|\,X=x_{0}\bigr)
. Therefore for all
\epsilon^{\prime}\in(0,1)
there exists
N_{\epsilon^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

I(t)\geq(1-\epsilon^{\prime})\lambda

for all
n\geq N_{\epsilon^{\prime}}
and
t\in[-1,1]
.
Define
\hat{t}_{n}:=\frac{\|K\|_{C_{\beta}}}{Lh^{\beta}K(0)}\hat{f}_{n}(x_{0})
. Then for
n\geq N\vee N_{\epsilon^{\prime}}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in\mathcal{H}(\beta,L)\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl\{\bigl(\hat{f}_{n}(x_{0})-f(x_{0})\bigr)^{2}\bigr\}\geq\sup_{t\in[-1,1]}\mathbb{E}_{P_{t}}\bigl\{\bigl(\hat{f}_{n}(x_{0})-f_{t}(x_{0})\bigr)^{2}\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\frac{L^{2}h^{2\beta}K^{2}(0)}{\|K\|_{C_{\beta}}^{2}}\sup_{t\in[-1,1]}\mathbb{E}_{P_{t}}\bigl\{(\hat{t}_{n}-t)^{2}\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\frac{K^{2}(0)}{\bigl(\|K\|_{C_{\beta}}^{2d}R_{2}(K)^{2\beta}\bigr)^{1/(2\beta+d)}}\biggl(\frac{L^{d/\beta}}{p_{X}(x_{0})i_{P}(x_{0})n}\biggr)^{2\beta/(2\beta+d)}\lambda^{2\beta/(2\beta+d)}\sup_{t\in[-1,1]}\mathbb{E}_{P_{t}}\bigl\{(\hat{t}_{n}-t)^{2}\bigr\}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{K^{2}(0)}{\bigl(\|K\|_{C_{\beta}}^{2d}R_{2}(K)^{2\beta}\bigr)^{1/(2\beta+d)}}\biggl(\frac{L^{d/\beta}}{p_{X}(x_{0})i_{P}(x_{0})n}\biggr)^{2\beta/(2\beta+d)}\sup_{m>0}\frac{\lambda^{2\beta/(2\beta+d)}}{(m+1)^{2}\{_{2}F_{1}\bigl(-\frac{1}{2},\frac{m}{2},\frac{m}{2}+1;-\frac{(1-\epsilon^{\prime})\lambda}{m^{2}}\bigr)\}^{2}},

by the augmented van Trees inequality
(
77
)
(restated as Theorem
34
for convenience) with
\alpha(t)=(1-|t|)^{m}
and

\pi(t)=\frac{\sqrt{\lambda\alpha^{2}(t)+\alpha^{\prime}(t)^{2}}}{\int_{-1}^{1}\sqrt{\lambda\alpha^{2}(\tau)+\alpha^{\prime}(\tau)^{2}}\,d\tau}.

Since
\epsilon^{\prime}\in(0,1)
and
\lambda>0
were arbitrary, we deduce that

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathrm{MSE}_{n}(\hat{f}_{n})

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\geq g\biggl(\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+d}\biggr)\biggl(\frac{(2\beta)^{2\beta}d^{d}}{(2\beta+d)^{2\beta+d}}\biggr)^{1/(2\beta+d)}\frac{K^{2}(0)}{\bigl(\|K\|_{C_{\beta}}^{2d}R_{2}(K)^{2\beta}\bigr)^{1/(2\beta+d)}}\biggl(\frac{L^{d/\beta}}{p_{X}(x_{0})i_{P}(x_{0})}\biggr)^{2\beta/(2\beta+d)},

where

g(\gamma):=\frac{1}{\gamma^{\gamma}(1-\gamma)^{1-\gamma}}\sup_{m>0}\sup_{\lambda>0}\frac{\lambda^{\gamma}}{(m+1)^{2}\bigl\{_{2}F_{1}\bigl(-\frac{1}{2},\frac{m}{2},\frac{m}{2}+1;-\frac{\lambda}{m^{2}}\bigr)\bigr\}^{2}}.

Now

\displaystyle g(\gamma)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\gamma^{\gamma}(1-\gamma)^{1-\gamma}}\sup_{m>0}\sup_{\lambda>0}\frac{4\lambda^{\gamma}}{(m+1)^{2}\bigl(\int_{0}^{1}t^{\frac{m}{2}-1}\sqrt{m^{2}+\lambda t}\,dt\bigr)^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\gamma^{\gamma}(1-\gamma)^{1-\gamma}}\sup_{m>0}\sup_{\lambda>0}\frac{m^{2}\lambda^{\gamma}}{(m+1)^{2}(m^{2}+\lambda)}=\sup_{m>0}\frac{m^{2\gamma}}{(m+1)^{2}}=\gamma^{2\gamma}(1-\gamma)^{2(1-\gamma)}.

This lower bound is convex in
\gamma
and symmetric about
1/2
, so

\inf_{\gamma\in(0,1)}g(\gamma)\geq\frac{1}{4},

which establishes (
94
) with the stated expression for
c_{\beta,d}(K)
and
a_{\beta,d}:=g\bigl(\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+d}\bigr)
. In fact, it can be verified numerically that

\inf_{\gamma\in(0,1)}g(\gamma)\geq\frac{1}{1.69}.

It remains to show the equality in (
22
). Take
\delta\in(0,1)
. Then, using the inequality
\sqrt{m^{2}+\lambda t\,}\geq(1-\delta)m+\delta\sqrt{\lambda t\,}
for
m,\lambda>0
and
t\in[0,1]
, we have for any
\gamma\in(0,1)
that

\displaystyle\sup_{\lambda>0}

\displaystyle\sup_{m>0}\frac{\lambda^{\gamma}}{(m+1)^{2}\bigl(\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}t^{\frac{m}{2}-1}\sqrt{m^{2}+\lambda t}\,dt\bigr)^{2}}\leq\sup_{\lambda>0}\sup_{m>0}\frac{\lambda^{\gamma}}{(m+1)^{2}\bigl((1-\delta)+\frac{\delta\sqrt{\lambda}}{m+1}\bigr)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\lambda>0}\sup_{m>0}\frac{\lambda^{\gamma}}{(1-\delta)^{2}(m+1)^{2}+\delta^{2}\lambda}=\sup_{\lambda>0}\frac{\lambda^{\gamma}}{(1-\delta)^{2}+\delta^{2}\lambda}=\frac{\gamma^{\gamma}(1-\gamma)^{1-\gamma}}{\delta^{2\gamma}(1-\delta)^{2(1-\gamma)}}.

Therefore

\gamma^{2\gamma}(1-\gamma)^{2(1-\gamma)}\leq g(\gamma)\leq\frac{1}{\delta^{2\gamma}(1-\delta)^{2(1-\gamma)}}.

In particular,

1\leq\lim_{\gamma\searrow 0}g(\gamma)\leq\frac{1}{(1-\delta)^{2}}.

As
\delta\in(0,1)
was arbitrary,

\lim_{\beta\searrow 0}\inf_{d\in\mathbb{N}}a_{\beta,d}=\lim_{\gamma\searrow 0}g(\gamma)=1,

which completes the proof.
∎

Theorem 34

(Augmented van Trees inequality,
77
)
.

Let
(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{A},\mu)
be a
\sigma
-finite measure space. Assume that:

(i)

p:\mathcal{X}\times[-1,1]\rightarrow[0,\infty)
is a measurable function such that
p(\cdot,t)
is a (Lebesgue) density for each
t\in T
, and that
t\mapsto p(x,t)
is absolutely continuous for
\mu
-almost all
x
. Further suppose that the Fisher information

\mathcal{I}(t):=\int_{\mathcal{X}}\biggl(\frac{\partial_{t}p(x,t)}{p(x,t)}\biggr)^{2}p(x,t)\,d\mu(x)

satisfies
\int_{-1}^{1}\mathcal{I}(t)\,dt<\infty
.

(ii)

\pi
is a (Lebesgue) density on
[-1,1]
.

(iii)

\alpha:[-1,1]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is absolutely continuous with
\alpha(-1)=\alpha(1)=0
and

\max\biggl\{\int_{-1}^{1}\alpha(t)\,dt,\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\alpha^{2}(t)}{\pi(t)}\,dt,\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\alpha^{\prime}(t)^{2}}{\pi(t)}\,dt\biggr\}<\infty.

Then writing
P_{t}
for the distribution with density
p(\cdot,t)
, we have for any measurable
\hat{t}:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
that

\int_{-1}^{1}\mathbb{E}_{P_{t}}\bigl\{\bigl(\hat{t}(X)-t\bigr)^{2}\bigr\}\pi(t)\,dt\geq\frac{\bigl\{\int_{-1}^{1}\alpha(t)\,dt\bigr\}^{2}}{\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\mathcal{I}(t)\alpha^{2}(t)+\alpha^{\prime}(t)^{2}}{\pi(t)}\,dt}.

Theorem
6
follows by applying Lemma
33
for a specific choice of bump function
K
.

Proof of Theorem
6
.

The result follows from Lemma
33
by taking
K(\nu)=\prod_{j=1}^{d}\bigl\{e^{-1/(1-\nu_{j}^{2})}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|v_{j}|<1\}}\bigr\}
. In that case,
K(0)=e^{-d}
,
R_{2}(K)\leq 2^{d}e^{-2d}<\infty
and
\|K\|_{C_{\beta}}
is finite and depends only on
(\beta,d)

(e.g.
47
, Exercise 8.13)
.
In the case
\beta\in(0,1]
, take
K=K^{*}
, where

K^{*}(\nu):=\max\bigl(1-\|\nu\|^{\beta},0\bigr).

(99)

Then
K^{*}(0)=1
. Moreover, for
u,v\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(1)
,

|K^{*}(u)-K^{*}(v)|=\bigl|\|u\|^{\beta}-\|v\|^{\beta}\bigr|\leq\bigl|\|u\|-\|v\|\bigr|^{\beta}\leq\|u-v\|^{\beta},

and if
u\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(1)
,
v\not\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(1)
, define
v_{\perp}:=\argmin_{\nu\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(1)}\|\nu-v\|
. Then

|K^{*}(u)-K^{*}(v)|=|K^{*}(u)-K^{*}(v_{\perp})|\leq\|u-v_{\perp}\|^{\beta}\leq\|u-v\|^{\beta}.

Therefore
\|K^{*}\|_{C_{\beta}}=1
. Finally,

R_{2}(K^{*})=\frac{2^{d+1}\beta^{2}}{(\beta+d)(2\beta+d)}\cdot\frac{\Gamma^{d}(1+1/q)}{\Gamma(1+d/q)}.

Thus

\displaystyle\biggl(\frac{(2\beta)^{2\beta}d^{d}}{(2\beta+d)^{2\beta+d}}\biggr)^{1/(2\beta+d)}\frac{K^{*2}(0)}{\bigl(\|K^{*}\|_{C_{\beta}}^{2d}R_{2}(K^{*})^{2\beta}\bigr)^{1/(2\beta+d)}}

\displaystyle=\biggl(\frac{(\beta+d)^{2\beta}d^{d}}{(2^{d}\beta)^{2\beta}(2\beta+d)^{d}}\cdot\frac{\Gamma^{2\beta}(1+d/q)}{\Gamma^{2\beta d}(1+1/q)}\biggr)^{1/(2\beta+d)}.

Applying Lemma
33
and recalling (
93
), we obtain (
94
) with

c_{\beta,d}(K^{*})=a_{\beta,d}C_{\beta,d},

so the result follows from (
95
).
∎
